# Changelog

The format is [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the versions are [semantic](https://semver.org/).

While the version is `0.x`, a minor version (`0.2` → `0.3`) may break the engine's API, and a patch (`0.2.0` → `0.2.1`) only fixes bugs.
From `1.0.0` on, breaking changes come only in a major version. All engine crates (`ai_core`, `shards_*`,
`shards`, `sandbox`, `shards_cli`) share one version: it is set once in `[workspace.package]` of the root
`Cargo.toml`. The Bevy version is pinned (`0.20.0`); updating it is a separate entry in the "Changed" section.

## How a game depends on the engine

A game depends on one crate, `shards`, and pins the engine to a **git tag**. This way the build does not change until
you change the tag yourself. To follow unreleased changes on the main branch, pin a commit instead: `rev = "<hash>"` (and `--rev <hash>` for the CLI).

```toml
# The game's Cargo.toml
[dependencies]
shards = { git = "https://github.com/larelgit/shards-engine", tag = "v0.4.0" }
```

Use the `shards` CLI of the same version as the engine in the game (the `ai/*` protocol may change between minor
versions):

```bash
cargo install shards_cli --git https://github.com/larelgit/shards-engine --tag v0.4.0
```

To move to a new version, change `tag`, run `cargo update -p shards`, read the "Changed" section of this
file, and run the game's tests and replays (`--verify`). Replays recorded on an old version may diverge after the engine's
gameplay changes; in that case, record them again.

## Releasing a version (for the maintainer)

Tags are set by the maintainer, not by agents or CI. For a release `X.Y.Z`:

1. Raise `version` in `[workspace.package]` of the root `Cargo.toml` and run `cargo update --workspace`
   (it updates `Cargo.lock`). Move the entries from `Unreleased` into a new section `[X.Y.Z] — date` and fix the
   links at the bottom of the file. The section may already be prepared (phase B is recorded in `[0.3.0]`, phases C–E in `[0.4.0]`): then set the
   date in it. A new release line rejects the snapshots and saves of the previous one (before 1.0 a line is a minor version, `0.4`;
   from 1.0 it is the major version, so `1.5` still reads `1.4`): record `tests/replays/snapshot.json` and `saves.json`
   again (the other replays do not change)
   (`cargo test -p sandbox --test sandbox -- --ignored record_reference_replays`).
2. Update the `vX.Y.Z` tag in `template/Cargo.toml` (the comment about the dependency), in `template/README.md` (steps 3 and 5), in
   `README.md` (step 2 of the section "Make a game on Shards, step by step") and in `docs/manual.md` (step 2 of the section "Make a game
   on Shards, step by step"), replacing `rev` with `tag`, and sync
   `template/Cargo.lock` with the root one (CI checks that the dependency versions in them do not diverge):
   `cp Cargo.lock template/Cargo.lock && cargo metadata --manifest-path template/Cargo.toml --format-version 1 >/dev/null`.
3. Make sure CI is green on the commit that becomes the release, and merge it into the main branch.
4. Create and push an annotated tag on this commit:

```bash
git checkout main && git pull
git tag -a vX.Y.Z -m "Shards X.Y.Z"
git push origin vX.Y.Z
```

The tag `v0.1.0` is not yet in the history; it points to commit `e90193d`, the last one before phase A:

```bash
git tag -a v0.1.0 -m "Shards 0.1.0" e90193d
git push origin v0.1.0
```

## [Unreleased]

Changes that land in the main branch after phases C–E (`0.4.0`) go here.

### Added
- A voice cut short (the manual's "Audio (E9.5)", "Voices"): `shards::audio::stop(world, id, fade)` stops the voice that
  `play` started (`Played::Started { id, .. }`) before it plays out. It fades to silence over `fade` seconds on its
  clock and is let go, and its subtitle goes at once; `0` stops it at once. `Voice::stopping` (`Fading`) holds the
  fade, and the `audio` tool marks the voice `(stopping)`. An emitter's voice is refused, since its emitter would take
  one again. For an interrupted line or a radio call cut off mid-word. Presentation only: the mixer is never in a
  snapshot or the replay hash. Tests: `a_voice_cut_short_fades_out_and_its_line_goes_at_once`, and the emitter's
  refusal in `an_emitter_starts_and_stops_by_its_flag_the_api_and_the_tools_and_goes_with_its_entity`.
- Lights from content, flicker, the shadow budget and colour grading (F13, the FPS spec; the manual's "Lights and colour grading (F13)", "Levels from Blender (E4.2)", "Camera numbers and offsets (C7)" and "Settings (E8.6)"). The new module `shards_light` (`shards::light`, optional: `--without light`) brings the content kind `light` (`*.light.ron`, `LightDef`, not a simulation kind: `kind: Point | Spot(inner_deg, outer_deg)` with the cone's whole opening in degrees, `color` (`#rrggbb`) or `kelvin`, `intensity` in Bevy's lumens, `range`, `radius`, `shadows`, `flicker: None | Pattern(pattern, rate) | Noise(rate, depth)`) and the reflected component `ContentLight { id }`, which a level node's new property `light=<id>`, a prefab (`"ContentLight": (id: "lamp")`) or code places. With `render` each content light gets a Bevy `PointLight` or `SpotLight` on a child entity without an `AiId` (`LightPicture`), driven every frame; headless the module keeps only its state (`LightState`). On a Blender lamp `light=` replaces the lamp: the glTF loader's own light under that node is taken out of the picture (`shards_level` render), so the file keeps a lamp for Blender and the game keeps the numbers in content; a game's other Bevy lights are left alone. A flicker is a pure function of the simulation's clock (`Time<Fixed>` elapsed plus the overstep, `shards_motion::camera::picture_clock`) and of a seed that is the light's own (its definition id, node name and place when it first appeared, kept while it is placed: a light that moves keeps its wander), never `SimRng`: a pause freezes it and `step` moves it, a headless game and a drawn one agree, and the player's `accessibility.flashes` shapes it (`reduced`: at most 2 steps or changes a second and half as far from the light as written; `off`: steady); a pattern is the classic light style (`a` dark, `m` as written, `z` about twice), noise is smooth value noise between `1 - depth` and 1. The shadow budget is the new `graphics.shadow_lights` setting (`u32`, default 4, 0 to 64, none under `graphics.shadows = "off"`): of the content lights that ask for a shadow, the nearest to the viewer (the `AiCamera`, else an orbit or first-person camera, else the local player's eye) cast one, ties by a key of the definition id, name and place, never `Entity` (`budget::casters`, pure). Colour grading: the camera content's `picture` gains `grading: Option<Grading>` (`exposure`, `temperature`, `tint`, `hue_deg`, `saturation`, `contrast`, `lift`, `gamma`, `gain`, neutral by default and left out of the written form, so no camera hash moves), Bevy's `ColorGrading` with the same values for shadows, midtones and highlights on every third-person and first-person camera (and, when the world camera does not draw in HDR, on the view model's camera: an HDR camera grades the finished picture the view model is drawn over, so the arms stay ungraded there); Bevy grades before tone mapping, so `tone_mapping: None` shows none of it, and per-channel wheels and a LUT are not in Bevy's built-in path. The new content kind `grading` (`*.grading.ron`: `GradingDef { blend_s, grading }`) and the component `GradingVolume { id }` (a level node's new property `grading=<id>` on a `marker = trigger` node, or a prefab) blend the picture toward a grade while the local player's body touches the volume (the camera's place when there is no local player; the last volume in collider id order wins), over `blend_s` (smoothstep, from what is shown, so nothing jumps), and back to the camera's own grade when it leaves: the resource `GradingBlend` is tracked in `FixedLast` on every tick and evaluated on the simulation's clock, and is in no snapshot or replay (`ContentLight` and `GradingVolume` are `replay_ignore`d: a trigger marker has an `AiId`). The content check names the file, the definition and the field (`intensity` 0 or more, `range` above 0, spot angles `0 < inner <= outer < 180`, a pattern of `a`-`z` only, `blend_s` 0 to 60, every grade field in its range) and the level check names the node (`light=` or `grading=` that names no definition with a "did you mean", an empty one, `grading` off a trigger marker). The player query `lights` (counts by kind, the flicker now, who casts a shadow against the budget and why not, the grade and its blend; `id=`, `limit=`) and `light` in `ai/status`. The sandbox has `content/light/` (the lights `lamp`, `sodium`, `tube`, `flasher`, `dimmer`, `torch`, the gradings `rust` and `cold`, the prefabs `lit_lamp` and `lit_flasher`, the level `lit_room` with `assets/levels/lit_room.gltf` written by `tests/sandbox/lights.rs`), the scenario `lights.scenario` (queries only, so it runs headless) and `docs/media/lights.png`; 297 definitions in 102 files; no reference replay changed. Frame time: `--perf reference --offscreen` passes every budget (tick average 0.87-1.14 ms against 0.85-0.98 ms for the main it was cut from, frame average 3.32-3.94 ms against 3.35-3.59 ms, three back-to-back runs each; the scene has no lights; the module adds two resources, which Bevy 0.20 keeps as entities: 16484 entities against 16482). Tests: `shards_light` 25 (`src/` 8: the definitions, the flicker and what the player's flash setting makes of it, the budget; `tests/light/` 17: the content check, the query on the clock, the flash setting, a moving noise light that keeps its wander (and stays within the reduced rate), the budget, the Bevy lights of a drawn game), `shards_motion` +7 (`camera/grading.rs`: the blend, the volume pick, the cameras it reaches), `shards` +1 (the view model's grade), the sandbox's `lights.rs` (6, and 4 `#[ignore]`d that need a GPU: the picture holds exactly the six content lights, a flashing lamp lights the room at one moment and not at the next, a grading volume changes the shot's channel means and gives them back, and the manual's picture, written with `SHARDS_WRITE_MEDIA=1`); measured on a GPU with `--offscreen` (RTX 5080): the flasher on against off moved the room's brightest block by 91 of 255 and the picture's mean luminance by 10, and the `rust` volume moved the mean colour from (85, 88, 78) to (98, 59, 56). Not here: directional lights as content, an emissive bulb that follows a flicker, hysteresis in the shadow budget.
- Use by looking (F5; the manual's "Use by looking (F5)" in "Actions and combat (E3)", and "Levels from Blender (E4.2)"): a character uses the thing its look meets by pressing `interact`. `shards_motion`: the physics layer `USE` (`LayerMask::USE`, `CollisionLayers::USE`: a query layer whose colliders collide with nothing: no body is stopped by one, a query meets one only when its mask has `USE` (the engine's own queries name the layers they want; `QueryFilter::default()` takes every layer), the navmesh does not carve one and a mover does not push with one), the component `Usable { name, prompt, hold_s, use_range }`, the pure ray `use_target(world, user)` (from `eye_of` along `look_dir` against `USE` and `WORLD` together: a target counts when it is nearer than every `WORLD` hit or at its distance, within its own `use_range` or the body's; the nearest wins and a tie goes to the lower `ColliderId`) and `use_probe` (everything the ray passes, up to 100 m, for refusals), `use_point`, and the `locomotion` field `use_range` (default 2 m, at most 100, left out of the written form at the default, so no content hash moves). `shards_action`: the set `ActionSystems::Use` (`SimSet::Act`, after the context and before the machine) serves the `interact` presses of bodies in `AiId` order: a press that finds a target takes the press and triggers `Used { who, what, name }` (journalled `e5 uses e12 "door"`; not a net event), a `hold_s` starts `UseProgress { what, elapsed }` (simulation state: snapshots, the replay hash, co-op) that grows while the button stays held and the look stays on the thing and ends in `Used`; letting go, looking away, going out of reach, going down or the thing going away cancels it. A revive keeps its press: a moveset whose context binds `interact` (a downed ally in reach) leaves it to the machine. The tool `interact` takes `target=eN` (turns the look to the thing's use area first; every refusal says why and leaves the look as it was: too far, behind a wall or glass, a nearer thing on the line, no use area), `hold=true` for a thing with a `hold_s`; `observe` shows `USE e12 "door" 1.4m` (with `hold 1.0s`, `, using 0.5/1.0s`); `use_view` for a HUD. `shards`: the UI sources `you.use_target` (the prompt, a Fluent `@id` resolved) and `you.use_progress` (a ratio). `shards_level`: an `interactable` marker gets `Usable` and a use area from its node (a node with its own `collider` stays solid and adds `USE`, so a door is a wall and a target; a mesh or a `size` without a `collider` gets an `auto` box on `USE` alone; a bare empty is a marker as before), the node properties `hold_s` (zero or more), `use_range` (above zero, at most 100) and `prompt` (a Fluent `@id` or text), the layer word `use`, checks that name the level, the node and the property, and `spawn_level` counts use areas apart from solids; and `despawn_level` (so `level_despawn` and `level_reload`) removes a level's entities in the file's node order instead of the order of a query, which followed the tables the entities sat in: the entities with an `AiId` leave tombstones in the order they go, and a snapshot's twin, which spawns them in another table order, buried them differently (heavy mode found it on the new replay). Not done: a use by an NPC, a sphere cast for small things, `Used` as a net event.
- Sandbox, for F5: the test room `use` (`assets/levels/use.gltf`, written by the tests in `tests/sandbox/using.rs`; `content/world/use.level.ron`: eight stations, one rule each), the scenario `use.scenario` and the reference replay `tests/replays/use.json` (twenty-six reference replays with F11's `npc_ranged.json` and F2's `crouch.json`; recording it left the twenty-four before it byte-identical, and every one verifies). Content: 275 definitions in 93 files with F11's, F2's and F1b's.
- Gore: marks on any surface and debris that lasts (F9, the FPS spec; the manual's "Gore: marks and debris (F9)" in "Effects and decals (E10.3)"). **Marks**: the content kind `mark` (`*.mark.ron`, picture only: `SIMULATION = false`) names a spot on a surface: a `texture` (a PNG inside the assets folder) or a plain soft spot, a `color`, a `size` range, a random turn (`rotate`), `fade_in` and `fade_out`, `lifetime: Secs(f32) | UntilCleared`, a `max_live` cap per kind (one more takes the place of the oldest laid), `depth`, `roughness`, `unlit` and `tags`. A mark is a `ForwardDecal` with Bevy's `StandardMaterial` (so it is lit), its quad turned so that its +Y is the surface's normal, so it lies on a wall, a ceiling or a floor (the telegraph decals' projector, with a material of its own); the cameras get the depth prepass while marks are laid. The game lays one with `shards::fx::spawn_mark(world, id, point, normal)` (`spawn_mark_seeded`) or `PlayMark`; the `fx` table's new map `marks` (`MarkLink { mark, place: At | Behind(m) | Below(m), count, spread, offset }`, left out of the written form when empty) lays them from `shot_impact` (a hole where a shot stopped), `shot_hit` (blood on the surface behind a body a shot went through: a ray along the shot, from where `ShotFired` says it started), `damaged`, `died` and `part_broken`. The table's `effects` gain the events `shot_impact`, `shot_hit` and `part_broken`, and `Aim` the variants `Away` and `Surface`. **Debris**: `EffectDef` gains `collide`, `restitution` (default 0.4), `friction` (0.6), `rest: Fade | UntilCleared`, `mesh` (a glTF per particle; the particle shader copes with a mesh that has no UVs), `image` (a PNG a `Quad` wears) and `tags`, all left out of the written form at their defaults, so existing effects, their content hashes and their pictures are unchanged. A landing particle is stepped (1/60 s on the simulation's clock, a ray a step through `PhysicsWorld::raycast` on `WORLD`, at most 2048 rays a frame), bounces, slides, follows the ground down a step, falls off an edge and comes to rest lying on its side; `UntilCleared` keeps it past the 30 s cap until it is cleared or the pool recycles it. **Clearing**: `shards_fx::clear(world, tag)`, `clear_all` and the tool `fx_clear tag=gore` (or `all=true`); a new world (`WorldRebuilt`: a region change, `regen`, a snapshot load) and leaving `InGame` clear everything by themselves. **For the AI**: the tool `mark_spawn` (a mark at a point and a normal, or where a ray finds a surface), `effects` lists the marks alive per kind and the debris at rest, `ai/status` `fx` gains `marks`, `debris` and `rays` (the gore's performance counters). All picture only: no `AiId`, nothing in a replay or a snapshot, nothing on a headless machine, and a game that draws plays the same game as one that does not (the state hash is the same); `ShotFired`, `ShotImpact`, `Damaged` and `Died` reach a co-op client from the host, so it lays the same marks (`PartBroken` is not a net event: chunks linked to `part_broken` show on the host and in solo play). `shards_action::view_model::leaves_the_assets` is public (the content checks of a picture path use it). Not in F9: marks on bodies and on moving surfaces.
- Sandbox, for F9: the marks `bullet_hole`, `blood_splat` and `blood_pool` (`content/fx/gore.mark.ron`, textures `textures/marks/*.png`), the effects `wall_chips`, `blood_spray` and `meat_chunk` (`gore.effect.ron`, a chunk mesh `models/chunk.glb`; `cargo run -p sandbox --example make_gore` writes the three files), the links in `sandbox.fx.ron`, the prefabs `gore_floor`, `gore_wall` and `gore_ceiling` (`gore.prefab.ron`, a test room), the scenario `gore.scenario` (queries and a few shots: a headless game lays nothing), `tests/sandbox/gore.rs`, and the picture `docs/media/gore-room.png`. `.gitignore` lets PNGs in `crates/*/assets/` in.
- The first-person camera, the hidden own body and the view model (F1b, the FPS spec; the manual's "The first-person camera and the view model (F1b)", "Camera numbers and offsets (C7)" and "Audio (E9.5)"). `FirstPersonCamera` (a component on the game's camera, or put on every `ThirdPersonCamera` entity by `camera` content's `mode: FirstPerson`, default `ThirdPerson`, so a game that never says it sees no change; `shards_motion::camera::first_person`) turns the local player's `Intent.look` from the `look` axis in `PreUpdate` (the player's `sensitivity`, `aim_sensitivity` while `CameraOffsets::aiming`, `invert_y`, the body's `pitch_limits_deg`; it writes only on a frame the axis moved, plus one seed of the body's heading when it first looks through a body that has no look, and starts from the look the intent holds, so an agent's `look`, a recorded input and a replay that plays are never overwritten) and sits at the eye of the interpolated body, turned by whatever `Intent.look` holds, in `PostUpdate`: the eye eased on steps (`first_person.step_ease`, 25 per second, while the body stands), a landing dip on the `Landed` event (a critically damped spring, `first_person.landing`), an optional walking bob and sideways lean (`bob`, `roll`, off by default), the shake and the game's `CameraOffsets`. `read_input` walks by the look yaw under it, whatever the body faces; the orbit camera and its lock-on stand still under it (the `lock_on` button still lets go of a lock that is held; a first-person camera counts as the local player's view only while it is on, `FirstPersonCamera::active`, and looks through the local player: another body's, a spectator's, or a switched-off one leaves the walk, the lock-on, the ears and the body's drawing alone; a scene shot holds the look input). New `camera` fields `mode` and `first_person` (all `skip_serializing_if` their defaults, checked: a default definition still serializes to `()`). The own body's meshes go to `OWN_BODY_LAYER` (62), which the camera, drawing layer 0, never draws; while a first-person camera exists lights and other 3D cameras with no `RenderLayers` get the layers (`[0, 62, 63]` and `[0, 62]`), because in Bevy a light lights a camera only if they share a layer and a shadow caster counts only for a light that shares its layer: the body still casts its shadow and a mirror or a minimap shows it. The view model is a second camera (`ViewModelCamera`, `VIEW_MODEL_LAYER` 63, one order above the world's, its own field of view and near plane, loads the world and clears the depth, copies its HDR, anti-aliasing and tone mapping; the HUD is over it) over a glTF rig (`ViewModelRig`) that the animation bridge animates from the **body's** actions through the new `ShowsBody(body)` (the bridge's `drive` reads the body's `ActionState`, `MotionState`, `Fighter` and the rest: no second fighter, no `AiId`, picture only; sockets, props and `@moveset` mounts work on it as on a body). New content kind `view_model` (`*.view_model.ron`: `model`, `animset`, `look`, `fov_deg`, `near`, `offset`, `scale`; `SIMULATION = false`) and `MovesetDef::view_model` (an id, left out when `None`, so no moveset hash moves): the weapon in hand names its view model and an `Arsenal` switch swaps it; `camera` content's `first_person.view_model` is the one for a body whose moveset names none. The audio listener of a body that is looked through in the first person (it faces its look, or a `FirstPersonCamera` looks through it) is at its eye (`eye_of`) instead of its feet, so the occlusion ray starts there (F10's note). The developer tool `camera_mode mode=first|third|content` chooses the camera for the session over the content. Latency, measured on a stepped clock with the window's own events (no OS input): the look turns the picture in the frame the mouse moves in; a click starts its action in the first fixed tick after the frame that reads it and the view model shows it in that frame, at most one tick and one frame (the manual's table). The sandbox has `models/view_model.glb` (written by `cargo run -p sandbox --example make_view_model`: a pair of arms and a weapon slab, six clips), the animset `arms`, the view model `arms`, the camera content that names it and the scenario `first_person.scenario`; 236 definitions in 76 files; no reference replay changed. Not built: the view from a bone (item 7, optional), the window's cursor grab (the game's).
- Air movement in the style of *Half-Life* (F7; the manual's "Movement (E2)", "Air movement in the style of Half-Life (F7)"). The `locomotion` option `accel` (`shards::motion::AccelModel`) picks how the ground and the air steer a body: `Step` (the default, left out of the written form: the controller runs exactly the code it ran, so content hashes and every reference replay are unchanged) or `Projected(ground_accel, air_accel, air_speed_cap, friction, stop_speed, max_air_speed)`, the model of *Quake* and *Half-Life* (a push along the wished direction is limited by the speed the body has already projected on it, so strafing turns the body in the air and a bunny hop keeps its speed; the FPS spec called it Quake). Every field has a default, Half-Life's numbers in metres (`ground_accel` and `air_accel` 10, `air_speed_cap` 0.762, `friction` 4, `stop_speed` 2.54, no `max_air_speed`). On the ground the friction takes `max(speed, stop_speed) * friction * dt` off, then the push is `min(wish_speed - v . wish_dir, ground_accel * wish_speed * dt)` along the wish; in the air there is no friction and the speed to be reached is held to `air_speed_cap` while the step is taken from the whole wish speed (as in the originals). Written in closed form per tick (`controller/accel.rs`, through `bevy::math::ops`): the friction is the exact solution of `dv/dt = -friction * max(v, stop_speed)` (an exponential above the stop speed, a line below it, a crossing inside a tick split), the push a clamped straight line; a steady or time-scripted wish ends at the same velocity at 30, 60, 120 and 240 ticks a second (a scripted strafe run within 3 cm and 2 cm/s), and the original's per-frame friction agrees within 3 % at 60 and 120. A strafe whose wish follows the velocity tick by tick gains speed that depends on the tick rate (second order in each nudge, the originals' frame-rate effect), which the manual states with numbers. Open question 3 (may a strafe jump pass the run speed?) is content's: `max_air_speed: None` lets it, `Some(m)` stops the air pushing a body past `m` (a faster take-off keeps its speed). It replaces the steering of the ground and of the air with input only (`acceleration`, `deceleration`, `air_control`, `brake_only_without_input` and the modifiers' rate overrides do not steer them under it, and `axis_stepping` only shapes the optional air drag; `air_deceleration` with no input, the wished speeds, the modifiers' caps, crouch, slopes, steps, movers, glide, swimming, `external` pushes and landings are as they were). No new state: `MotionState` and `LocomotionInput` are unchanged, nothing to snapshot or register for replays. `move_info` gives `accel` (the model and its numbers) and `speed` (the horizontal speed). Checked with `accel: ground_accel must be > 0` style messages; a typo is an error with its line.
- Sandbox, for F7: the locomotion `air_viewer` (`characters/air_move.locomotion.ron`: the first-person `viewer` with Half-Life's numbers written out), the prefab `air_runner` (`characters/air_move.prefab.ron`), and the scenario `air_move.scenario` (on the canyon floor: the run-up and the stop by friction, the air's cap on a sideways push, air braking, a bunny hop that keeps its speed, the tool's numbers).
- Crouching (F2; the manual's "Movement (E2)", "Crouching (F2)"). A body whose `locomotion` gives a `crouch_height` lowers its capsule while the new held input `crouch` (`input_names::CROUCH`, `Intent::held`; the standard map binds it to X, `accessibility.hold_to_toggle` turns it into a toggle) is held; `crouch_speed` caps the crouched ground walk (and a crouched body never sprints) and `crouch_time` is how long a full crouch or stand takes (0: at once). All three are left out of the written form at their defaults, so no content hash moves, and are checked with the field named; the eye keeps its place in the capsule (`CharacterParams::eye_above_feet_at`), so `eye_of` (the shot's origin, the `AIM` line, a first-person camera) follows the stance. On the ground the feet stay and the head comes down; in the air the head stays and the feet are pulled up, so a crouch in a jump clears a ledge a plain jump misses. A stand-up is a shape cast of the standing capsule (up on the ground, down in the air) and waits while it is blocked: no body stands into a ceiling or grows into a floor. The stance is the reflected component `Crouch { depth, wanted, blocked }` (snapshots, the replay hash, `inspect`, co-op, `net_predicted`) beside `MotionState`, put on a body the first time it crouches: a field on `MotionState` or `LocomotionInput` would have changed the hash of every body in every recording, and with this the twenty-four reference replays recorded before verify unchanged. Events `Crouched` and `Stood` (journal `e5 crouches`, `e5 stands up`). `CharacterParams::{can_crouch, height_at, eye_above_feet_at, at_depth}` give every reader of a body's size the stance: the controller's casts and collider (a fighter's own collider is its hurtbox, so it shrinks with the capsule), the separation pass, the movers that carry and push it (F6) and `clearance`; `PartDef::at_crouched` (R1) moves a part with the stance so a head stays a head; `motion.finite` also judges `Crouch::depth`. For the AI: the player tool `crouch hold=true|false` (the answer says how far the capsule goes and over how long, and when there is no room to stand), the stance in the body's line (`ground 2.1m/s crouched`, `crouched, no room to stand`), `move_info`, and `stance=standing|crouched|current` on `clearance` and `path_to` (a crouched route is planned on the local grid: the navmesh is baked for the standing body). Not followed by the stance yet: the lock-on attack assist's line of sight, a leap's landing probe and an NPC's own eye.
- Sandbox, for F2: the locomotion `crouch_viewer` (`characters/crouch.locomotion.ron`), the prefab `crouch_dummy` (`characters/crouch.prefab.ron`) with its parts (`combat/crouch.parts.ron`, a head at 1.6 m and at 0.95 m crouched), the test room `crouch` (`assets/levels/crouch.gltf`, written by the tests in `tests/sandbox/crouch.rs`; `content/world/crouch.level.ron`: a duct only a crouch passes and a ledge only a crouch jump clears), the scenario `crouch.scenario` and its reference replay `tests/replays/crouch.json` (verified with `--heavy 10`). Content: 272 definitions in 90 files (with F11's). Reference replays: 25.
- Movers (F6; the manual's "Movers (F6): doors, lifts, platforms" in "Movement (E2)"): doors, lifts and platforms that go through keys, carry what stands on them and push what is in their way. `shards_motion`: the content kind `mover` (`MoverDef { keys, speed, turn_speed, wait, loop, blocked }`, `MoverKey { at, turn, time, wait }` relative to where the mover was placed and in its frame, `MoverLoop::{Off, PingPong, Cycle}`, `Blocked::{Stop, Reverse, Crush { damage, every }}`; the check names the file and the field: `keys[1]: the way from key 0 takes no time; it moves 1.00 m: give the mover a speed`, and refuses a way shorter than `MIN_WAY`, 0.01 s, so a step passes a bounded number of keys and runs all of its time), the components `Mover { def }`, `MoverPart { mover, offset }` and `MoverState` (in snapshots, co-op and the replay hash), the step `MoverSystems::Step` in `SimSet::Move` before the controller, in `AiId` order (a body standing on a mover is carried by its motion, its yaw turned with a turning one; a body it sinks into is pushed out the shortest way; one that cannot be blocks the step and the `blocked` rule acts; at rest the colliders are `Body::Static`, moving `Body::Kinematic`; a body keeps only its own velocity; movers push characters only), `mover_go`, `mover_state`, `MoverInfo`, the events `MoverStarted`, `MoverArrived`, `MoverBlocked`, the describer (`e14 Mover "Lift"  key 0→1, moving up 1.2m/s`), the dev tool `mover_go` (`id` or `name`, `key`), the condition `mover_arrived`, journal lines for movers that go only where they are sent, the engine rule `motion.mover_sane`, and the client's prediction of its own body on a mover run on to the predicted tick (`MoverSystems::Predict`, in `NetSet::Predict`; the host stays authoritative). `shards_action`: a crushing mover's damage through `deal_damage`, on the machine that simulates, with no credit. `shards_level`: the node property `mover=<id>` (the node's children in the file move with it as its parts; a mover inside a mover and an unknown definition are problems with the node named), the picture of a level mover follows it (`MoverPicture`, feature `render`), blended between ticks where the game simulates (alone and on a co-op host; on a client it follows the host's poses as they come), and the dev tool `level_despawn` (`id` or `instance`: a level `spawn_level` placed taken away; a region's tiles and a level the game placed itself are refused). `shards_npc`: a closed door is carved out of the navmesh at its next refresh, as any laid solid is (test only: no code change). Not done: a lift as a navigation link, momentum handed on, movers pushing props.
- Sandbox, for F6: the test room `movers` (`assets/levels/movers.gltf`, written by the tests in `tests/sandbox/movers.rs`; `content/world/movers.level.ron` and `movers.mover.ron`: a lift and the deck it serves, a gate that turns back from a body (`Reverse`), a crusher (`Crush`), a turntable (`Cycle`)), the scenario `movers.scenario` (it lays the room on the canyon floor, rides the lift up and down, is pushed by the gate, crushed and carried round, and takes the room away) and the reference replay `tests/replays/movers.json` (twenty-three reference replays with F4's `weapons.json`, F1a's `look.json` and F3's `hitscan.json`; recording it left the nineteen before them byte-identical, and every one verifies after the merges). Content: 247 definitions in 78 files. Engine rules: 33 with F4's `action.arsenal_sane` (the sandbox's `assert_list`: 35 rules with the game's two).
- Placed sounds and walls in audio (F10, the FPS spec; the manual's "Audio (E9.5)", "Looped sounds and emitters (F10)" and "Walls between the ears and a sound (occlusion, F10)"). `SoundDef::looped` (left out of the serialized form when false, so existing content is unchanged): a looped sound plays over and over and only from a `SoundEmitter { sound, playing }`, a reflected component a level node (`SoundEmitter = {"sound": "hum"}`), a prefab or code places (`SoundEmitter::new`, `emitter_play`, `emitter_stop`; the dev tools `emitter_play` and `emitter_stop` take a name, an id or the sound). The mixer holds one voice for an emitter while the listener is within the sound's `far`, lets it go beyond (no dropped sound), and takes one again when the listener is back; a one-shot of a lower priority never takes a loop's voice, a stronger one does and the emitter asks again; refreshing again with nothing changed starts nothing. A looped sound is refused as a one-shot (`play`, `sound_play`, `PlaySound`, a cue) and `--check-content` refuses a mixer event, a telegraph or a `Sound` effect that names one, a looped sound with a `subtitle`, `speaker` or `max_instances`, and a `SoundEmitter` in a prefab or a level node whose sound is missing or does not loop. Walls are opt-in: the mixer's `occlusion: Some((gain: 0.3))` (`AudioDef::occlusion`, `OcclusionDef`, absent by default and left out of the serialized form) casts a `WORLD` ray from the listener to each spatial voice through `PhysicsWorld` (read only); each collider on the way lets through its `SoundTransmission(f32)` (a reflected component, so a level node sets it) or the mixer's gain, the shares multiply, and the voice's `gain` carries the result (`Voice::occlusion`, `Occluded`, `Blocker`, `occlusion::measure`). The listener's own body, the source's entity, characters, sensors, a collider within 5 cm of the ears (the floor under the feet) and the last 10 cm before the source are not counted. `audio` lists the emitters (heard, waiting for a voice, out of earshot, stopped, or why one cannot play) and each voice's occlusion (`gain 0.10 prio 40 (loop) (occluded: Wall e15 Ã0.30)`); `sound_play` says what muffles its voice; `local` journal lines say when an emitter comes into and leaves earshot. Playback loops a looped voice's sink. Nothing of it is in the replay hash or snapshots (`SoundEmitter` and `SoundTransmission` are `replay_ignore`d), `shards_audio` depends directly on `shards_motion` and `ron` now, and no reference replay changed. Muffled voices are quieter, not duller (Bevy 0.20's sinks have no per-voice filter; the gap is written in the manual); the listener stays the body until the first-person camera (F1b) puts it at the eye. Sandbox, for F10: the looped sound `room_hum` (`ambient.sound.ron`, `sounds/room_hum.wav` from `make_sounds`, a seamless hum), the level `audio_room` (`tiles/audio_room.gltf` written by hand, `audio_room.level.ron`: a wall, a pane with a transmission of 0.6 and four emitters), the prefabs `audio_wall`, `audio_pane` and `audio_hum`, `tests/sandbox/ambient.rs`, and `ambient_audio.scenario` (hums in earshot and out of it, the walls with the mixer asking through `content_apply`, emitters started and stopped by name, a looped sound refused as a one-shot); 230 definitions in 71 files.
- Look as input (F1a; the manual's "Movement (E2)", "Look as input (F1a)"): `Intent.look: Option<Gaze>` (`shards::core::Gaze { yaw, pitch }` in radians: yaw 0 looks along -Z and a positive yaw turns left, a positive pitch looks up; `dir`, `flat_dir`, `toward(from, to)`, `with_pitch_between`, `from_degrees`) is where a character looks, written between ticks by whatever looks and recorded like every other input; in F1a that is the `look` tool, and the first-person camera that will write it from the mouse is F1b. The type is `Gaze` and not `Look` because `shards_look::Look` is a component that prefabs and levels name by its short name, and a second reflected type of that name would make `"Look": ("player")` ambiguous. `Intent` is reflected through its own serde form (`#[reflect(Serialize, Deserialize)]`) and leaves `look` out while it is `None`, so a replay check, a snapshot, `inspect` and a recorded input of a run that never looks are byte for byte what they were: the nineteen reference replays verify unchanged and a snapshot written before the field loads. The co-op mirror `IntentState::look` carries it (the host drops angles that are not finite), and the engine rule `core.intent_sane` names a `look` that is not finite. `locomotion` gains `face` (`Move`, the default, or `Look`: the body's yaw is the look's yaw every tick, with no turn rate, whatever it walks, climbs or runs; its aiming actions start along it, its tracking runs and backstep follow it, and the leap search and a client's prediction ask the same `machine::looked_at`; the keyboard and the stick walk relative to the view: `read_input` turns their axes by the yaw of the local player's look when its body faces its look, by the third-person camera's yaw otherwise, while `Intent.move_dir` stays in the world's directions, which the `move` tool and the orders write), `pitch_limits_deg` (default `(-89, 89)`) and `eye_height` (default 0.9 of `height`), all left out of the written form at their defaults, so no content hash moves; `CharacterParams::{eye_above_feet, pitch_limits, clamp_look, bound_look}` and the `Facing` enum are public. `shards::motion::{eye_of, look_dir, aim_point_of, params_of}` give every system the eye, the look direction (the clamped look, else the way the body faces, level) and the aim point of a body. For the AI: the player tool `look` (`yaw=` and `pitch=` in degrees, `at=eN [part=NAME]` toward an entity's aim point or the centre of one of its body parts, `clear=true`; it acts through `Intent` and a dead body is refused), the `AIM` line under `YOU` in `observe` while the subject has a look (`AIM e7 Dummy head 14.2m`, `AIM wall 3.1m`, `AIM nothing within 100m`: a ray from the eye along the look, 100 m, against the world, the bodies and the hurtboxes, its own left out, through a body's capsule to the part inside it; `shards::action::view_hit`) and `look W yaw 90.0° pitch 10.0°` in a body's line. The sandbox has the locomotion `viewer`, the prefabs `viewer` and `range_dummy` (`characters/look.prefab.ron`), the parts `range_dummy` (`combat/dummy.parts.ron`) and the scenario `look.scenario` with its reference replay `tests/replays/look.json` (content: 229 definitions in 71 files, the weapons slice's included; 21 reference replays). Tests: `intent.rs` (7), `params.rs` (4), `eye.rs` (3), `machine.rs` (1), `tests/motion/input.rs` (1: the walk turns by the look), `shards_net::inputs` (1), `tests/sandbox/look.rs` (9: a view goes through any number of bodies that take no hits, a run with looks replays identically and continues alike from its snapshots at `--heavy 30`, a client's look reaches the host and turns its body on both) and `snapshots.rs` (1). Not in F1a: the first-person camera, the hidden own body and the view model.
- Weapons: slots, switching, ammo, reload (F4; the manual's "Weapons: slots, switching, ammo, reload (F4)"). Ammo is the engine's named consumables: a magazine and its reserve are two `Charges` (`pistol`, `pistol.reserve`). `Cost::charge_count` (default 1, left out when serialized) is how many of `cost.charge` a run takes (a shotgun's two shells); a charge turn pays the difference when both actions take one charge. The new effect `Effect::Refill { charge, from, per_round }` moves up to the room `charge` has from `from` at its moment, so a run cut before it (a stagger, a switch through a cancel window) loads nothing; `per_round` moves one (a shell-by-shell reload is one effect per moment, or one in a `hold` loop); `Refilled` says what moved (`e5 refilled pistol +6 (6/6), 6 pistol.reserve left`). The start rule says what to do: `Blocker::Dry` (`pistol empty: reload`, or `pistol empty: no pistol.reserve left`) for a moveset with an action that refills the charge, `Blocker::NothingToRefill` (`pistol is already full (6/6)`) for a reload that would move nothing; a charge with no reload keeps `no flask charges left (0/3)` byte for byte. `MovesetDef::auto_reload: "action"` starts the reload instead of the press that cannot pay, when it would move something (`can_start`, so the tools and a client's prediction see it). `MovesetDef::weapon: (name, magazine, reserve, charges: {"pistol": (max, start)})` names the weapon's magazine and reserve and the charges a body gets with it. The component `Arsenal { slots, current, pending, bare }` (reflected, in snapshots and the replay hash; the module `shards_action::arsenal`) holds up to nine weapons by slot; the weapon in the hands is the body's `Fighter::moveset`, changed the way any moveset change is (draw and holster pictures keep working). The inputs `weapon.slot.N`, `weapon.next`, `weapon.prev` and `reload` (`shards_core::input_names`) ask for a switch, which `ActionSystems::Weapons` (first in `SimSet::Act`) makes once nothing runs or a cancel window naming `weapon` (or `Any`) is open, cutting the action; a refused one is journaled (`weapon.slot.7 rejected: no weapon in slot 7; you carry: 1 pistol`). The standard input map is unchanged (the wheel stays `lock_switch`): a game without lock-on binds `weapon.next` and `weapon.prev` in its own map. The calls a pickup makes: `grant_weapon` (`Grant::{Added, Replaced, AlreadyHeld}`), `take_weapon`, `select_slot`, `add_to_charge` (returns what it took), `refill`, `weapon_in_hands`. For agents: the player tools `reload` and `weapon slot=N|next|prev`, the dev tools `give_weapon`, `take_weapon` and `give_ammo`, the describer line `weapon pistol 6/6 (24) · slots 1 pistol, 2 blaster`, the journal lines `e5 switches to blaster (slot 2, from pistol)`, the UI sources `you.ammo`, `you.reserve` and `you.weapon`, and the engine rule `action.arsenal_sane`. Content checks (file and field named): `charge_count` below 1 or without a `charge`, a `Refill` with an empty name or one charge on both sides, an `auto_reload` that does not exist or refills nothing, a weapon whose actions cost or refill a charge it does not declare (neither `weapon.charges` nor a prefab's `Charges`), two weapons that declare one charge with different maxima, and a prefab `Arsenal` with a slot outside 1 to 9, a repeated slot, an unknown moveset or a `current` that holds nothing. The sandbox has two neutral test weapons (`test_sidearm`, `test_blaster`), the prefab `armed` and `weapons.scenario` (also the reference replay `weapons.json`, the 21st with F1a's `look.json`). Every new field is off by default and left out when serialized, so the content hashes and the reference replays are unchanged.
- Shooting: hitscan and 3D aim (F3; the manual's "Shooting: hitscan and 3D aim (F3)"). The new effect `Effect::Fire(FireDef)` fires a shot at its moment: `pellets` instant rays (default 1) from the shooter's eye along its look, pitch included, each turned within `spread_deg` (a uniform cone, 0 to 90), reaching `range` (default 100 m), keeping the `falloff` share of damage and poise by the distance flown (`(metres, share)` points, interpolated), passing `pierce` bodies, heard `noise` metres around, with `aim_error_deg` for a target-aimed shooter and `kick: (pitch, yaw)` recoil added to the shooter's `Intent.look` after the shot; its payload is a hit window's (`damage`, `poise`, `status`, `flags`, `tags`, `reaction`, `knockback`, `guard_damage`), and every field but `damage` is left out at its default. A ray stops at the first `WORLD` collider that is no body; the shooter, its parts and an object's owner are never met, a part stands for its body (the first part a ray goes through is the part hit; `body_hurtbox: false` bodies only through a part), allies, the dead, the intangible and a pair's held victim are looked through. Each pellet goes through the damage pipeline of a swing (credit, part factors, guards, parries, i-frames, armour, hooks, rules, reactions, poise, statuses) inside `ActionSystems::Effects` (`AiId` order, before this tick's movement); it hits a body at most once and ignores the run's "once per action" ledger, so pellets add up. Pellets draw from a keyed stream (seed, tick, `AiId`, the shot's ordinal this tick, the pellet), so a scatter gun spreads the same in every run, replay and heavy twin. The pure `shot_ray(world, shooter)` / `shot_aim` (the eye along the look; a body without a look toward its target's aim point, a part's centre for a lock point) and `trace_shot` are for any system that aims (F11's line of fire); `view_hit` now walks the same way. `combat.rs` factors the landing of a hit (credit to apply) into one path shared by hit windows and pellets: `DamageCtx` gains `guard_damage` (the guard reads it instead of the hit window's) and `shot: Option<ShotHit { pellet, distance, share, dir }>` (`hit` is `usize::MAX` for a pellet). The events `ShotFired` and `ShotImpact` (co-op net events) say where a shot went and what each pellet met; the NPC module turns `ShotFired::noise` into a `Noise` where the simulation runs. `SpawnAim::Look` lays a spawn along the shot ray (from the eye, `at` in the ray's frame, a projectile flying along it, pitch included), with `SpawnDef::aim_error_deg` for a target-aimed spawner. For agents: the player tool `fire [alt=true] [hold=true|false]` (refused with the action's name when the weapon fires nothing), the player query `shot_trace [id=eN] [input=light]` (the centre ray's bodies, parts, distances, falloff shares and stop, without firing), the `observe` line `SHOT rifle_shot -> e7 Dummy head 14.8m (x0.88)`, and one journal line per shot (`e5 fires rifle_shot: e7 head -48 hp (hp 52)`, `e5 fires scatter_shot (8 pellets): e7 torso, head -54 hp ×8 (hp 6)`, `... wall 20.0m`), into which the pellets' hit, block, parry and evade lines are folded. Content checks (file and field named): `pellets` 0, `spread_deg` or `aim_error_deg` outside 0..90, a `range` not above 0, a `falloff` that is not monotonic, negative numbers, `LineOfSight` on a shot, an unknown status, a spawn's `aim_error_deg` outside 0..90 or without `aim: Look`. The sandbox has four neutral test guns (`hitscan_rifle`, `hitscan_scatter`, `hitscan_piercer`, `hitscan_launcher`; the sandbox now has 242 definitions in 76 files), `hitscan.scenario` and its reference replay `hitscan.json` (the 22nd, also checked every 10 ticks by heavy mode). Every existing definition, replay and hash is unchanged. Not built: a co-op guest's kick (the guest's next input frame replaces the host's kicked look), hit markers and tracers in a window.
- NPCs: gunfire, hiding, ranged fighters, turrets (F11, items 1-4 of the FPS spec; the manual's "Gunfire, hiding, ranged fighters, turrets (F11)"). **Threats**: the perception option `notice: Threats` (default `Hostile`, left out when written) makes a non-combatant notice only the bodies it holds for a threat, whatever their team: one it saw attack while armed (an action of its own that fires a shot, or one with hit windows while it holds a weapon) or heard fire a shot (`ShotFired` within `noise` × `hearing`), for `memory` seconds, in the new component `NpcThreats` (only on such NPCs); the condition `threat_known`. **Hiding**: `shards::npc::hiding_spot(world, npc, threat_eye, within)` and `hiding_search` (the navmesh nodes reached from the NPC by a breadth-first walk within `within` m and its leash, one per metre, farthest from the threat first, ties by node id; the first the threat cannot see, by `WORLD` rays from its eye to the NPC's eye and body middle there; at most 4096 nodes and 96 rays; none off the navmesh); the hooks `hide` (runs there and stays, checks the spot each tick, searches again at most every `every` s when the threat sees it; with no spot it cowers) and `cower`, the condition `no_hiding_spot`. **Ranged fighters**: `AttackPlanDef::keep_range: Option<(min, max)>` keeps the NPC in its band (backs off, closes in, holds or moves in its plan's `wait` role inside it, also while its attack is in flight; no slot of the director's roles), and an option that fires a shot has the band as its default window; every tick after the brains each such NPC traces its line of fire to its target (`shards::action::line_of_fire`: target, ally or wall) into `NpcFireLine`, and while an ally or a wall is in the way its firing options are refused by the new rule `Refusal::LineOfFire { by }` (after `Machine`: fifteen rules), so no token is granted and nothing is pressed (`npc_schedule`: `no line of fire: e9 in the way`, `line_of_fire`). **Accuracy**: `FireDef::aim_error_moving` (the cone at a run, by the shooter's speed share) and `aim_error_far` (`(metres, factor)` points), both for a target-aimed shooter, checked. **Turrets**: the content kind `turret` (`turn_rate_deg`, `yaw_deg`, `pitch_deg`, `input`, `on_target_deg`, `range`), the component `Turret { def, active, rest }` on an NPC whose locomotion cannot walk: after the NPCs' intents it turns its look toward its target at the content rate within its limits from its rest and fires through F3 when its look is on the target and the line is clear; `set_turret_active(world, entity, on)` (event `TurretSwitched`, journal `e9 turret on`). For agents: the describer `npc_ranged` (`hiding`, `cowering`, `keeping range 8–14m`, `no line of fire: e9 in the way`, `turret tracking e5 (yaw 12°/60°)`, `(…, at its limit)`, `turret off`), `npc_state` `threats`, `hiding` (the spot), `range`, `turret`, `perception.notice`, the dev tool `turret_active [id=eN] on=` (every turret without an id), the conditions `npc_hidden` and `npc_line_blocked`. The sandbox has neutral content for it (`**/ranged.*.ron`: a bystander, gunners, a post, a turret, a wall; 268 definitions in 86 files with the movers'), `npc_ranged.scenario` and its reference replay `npc_ranged.json` (the 24th, also checked every 10 ticks by heavy mode). Every existing definition, replay and hash is unchanged. Not built: guards that move between cover points (item 5).
- Scenes that keep the player's camera, and NPC sequences (F12, the FPS spec; the manual's "Control lock (F12)" and "Sequences: scripted scenes for NPCs (F12)"). **Control lock**: `shards::motion::ControlLocks` on a body (a `ControlLock { movement, actions, look: Free | Cone { at: Target, deg } }` for each reason, sorted by reason; absent while nothing is locked, so every existing hash, snapshot and replay is unchanged), `lock_controls(world, entity, reason, lock)` and `unlock_controls(world, entity, reason)`, the events `ControlsLocked` and `ControlsUnlocked`. It is a filter on `Intent` inside the tick: `MotionSystems::Lock` (`SimSet::Act`, before the lock-on switch, `MotionSystems::Intent`, the weapons and the machine) takes the locked movement (`move_dir`, `jump` and `dodge`, `sprint` and `glide`) and actions (every other press and hold) out of `Intent` and clamps the look into its cones (a disc of `deg` degrees in yaw and pitch round the direction from the eye to a point or a body, written back into `Intent.look` so the first-person camera never winds up outside), and `MotionSystems::Unlock` (`FixedLast`, before the replay check) gives back what it took, because the input map writes only differences (a held key must come back when the lock lifts); the stash `LockStash` is empty between ticks. For agents: the `CONTROL looking only (scene)` line of `observe`, the dev tools `lock_controls` and `unlock_controls`, refusals of the player's movement and combat tools that say why (`your movement is locked (scene)`), journal lines, the engine rule `motion.control_lock_sane`. **Sequences**: the content kind `sequence` (`SequenceDef { roles, steps }`, steps `MoveTo`, `Face`, `Play`, `Sound`, `Wait`, `WaitFor`, `Signal`, each on a role's NPC, with `timeout`s; checked: declared and used roles, numbers, `Play` actions, `Sound` sounds that exist and do not loop), `shards::npc::play_sequence(world, id, &[(role, entity)]) -> Result<SequenceId, String>` and `stop_sequence`, the resource `Sequences` (snapshot state, the replay check `sequences`) and the component `SequenceHold` on the bodies that act. Steps run in `NpcSystems::Decide` (after the behaviour system) in run-number order, in whole ticks, and write the held NPC's `NpcDrive`, so its own navmesh route, avoidance and steering walk it; `Play` is `force_action`, `Sound` triggers `ActionSound` (the audio module plays it with its subtitle and speaker as for an action's sound), `WaitFor` asks the condition registry; a snapshot loaded in the middle of a step goes on with it. While a run holds an NPC its brain is dormant: `decide` leaves its brain, plan and timers as they were, the director, the planner and the combo continuation count it as under orders (a token comes back), its turret and line of fire stand down, its perception goes on, and it is awake wherever it stands; at the end the brain takes up from its state. A run that cannot go on (a marker, a body that died or went, a step past its `timeout`) ends with `SequenceEnded { skipped: true, why }` and a warning. Events `SequenceStarted`, `SequenceStep`, `SequenceSignal`, `SequenceEnded`. For agents: the dev tools `sequence_play id= role.NAME=eN|name` and `sequence_stop`, the query `sequences`, the condition `sequence_playing`, a journal line for each step, the `SEQUENCE` lines of `observe`, the NPC's `in sequence` fragment, `npc_state.sequence`, the engine rule `npc.sequence_holds`. The sandbox has neutral content for it (`content/scene/*.ron` and `levels/scene.gltf`, written by `tests/sandbox/sequence.rs`: a guide, a nod, a line with a subtitle and a speaker, a level with a marker; 304 definitions in 109 files with every other slice's), `sequence.scenario` and its reference replay `sequence.json` (the 28th). Every existing definition, replay and hash is unchanged. Not built: steps that run side by side on two NPCs, a `Sound` that waits for its line, the look nudge toward a point (picture only), the lock in a co-op client's own prediction.
- Effects that answer a blow and a body (the manual's "Effects and decals (E10.3)"): the particle shape `Disc` (a flat round disc `size` across that lies level and never turns: a shockwave, a ripple); `EffectDef::size_ease` (the size goes `1 − (1 − t)^size_ease` of the way to `size_end`: a ring that opens fast and slows) and `swell` (the size times `1 + swell × sin(π t)` on top: dust that billows before it thins); `unlit` (a cube, a shard or a disc in its own colour, unshaded, blended over what is behind rather than added as `glow` adds it); `push` (the share of a play's push each particle adds to its velocity) and `strength: StrengthResponse { count_per, speed, size }` (a burst of `count + count_per × (strength − 1)` particles, rounded down, each faster and bigger with the strength as it says; left out, the number and the size in proportion, as before). A play takes them through `play_with(world, effect, at, PlayOptions { toward, strength, push })`, `play_quietly_with`, `PlayEffect::strength` and `PlayEffect::push`, and `effect_play strength= push=`; what a `rate` emits later takes the same push and strength. Every new field is off by default and left out when serialized, so existing effects start the very particles they did (a play without a push draws no number for it; tested against the old maths). The content check names a `size_ease` that is not above 0, a negative `swell`, a `push` range out of order and a `count_per` that is no number. Sandbox: `guard_ring` (a `Disc` on `guard_broken`) and `kick_dust` (`swell`, `push`, a `strength` response, played by hand with a push), `effects.scenario` extended. Tests: `shards_fx` (`src/`: the strength response, the size curve, a play's scaling and push, the disc mesh and the `unlit` material; `tests/fx/`: the number, the speed and the size of a play, a disc drawn level, the push of a burst and of what a rate adds, a legacy play identical to the old maths, `effect_play strength` refused below 0).
- Light past white (the manual's "Camera numbers and offsets (C7)", "The picture", and "Effects and decals (E10.3)"): `CameraTuning::picture` (`PictureTuning { tone_mapping, bloom }`, in the non-simulation `camera` kind) gives every 3D camera with `ThirdPersonCamera` a tone mapping (`ToneMapping`, Bevy's operators) and a bloom (`BloomTuning { intensity, low_frequency_boost, low_frequency_boost_curvature, high_pass_frequency, threshold, threshold_softness, composite }`, defaults Bevy's natural bloom; `BloomComposite::{EnergyConserving, Additive}`); a bloom makes the camera draw in HDR. Both are left out by default and then the camera keeps what the game spawned it with; taking one out of the content gives the camera back its own (and turns HDR off again if the content turned it on); a camera spawned later gets the picture too. `EffectDef::intensity` (default 1, left out when serialized) multiplies an effect's light (linear, after sRGB) in the particles' shader: above 1 a colour goes past white, which a camera with HDR keeps and blooms; `effects` writes it as `x3`. Under HDR the particles are tone mapped by the camera like everything else (their shader has none of its own). The content check names a negative `intensity` and a bloom number out of range. Sandbox: `combat/camera.camera.ron` has a bloom with threshold 1.5 (only light 1.5 times past white glows: nothing lit by the sun) and `hit_spark` an `intensity` of 3; `effects.scenario` checks both. Replays are unchanged (`camera` and `effect` are not simulation kinds). Tests: `shards_motion` `camera::picture` (a picture left out keeps the camera, a bloom draws in HDR and giving it back restores the camera, a game's own bloom and HDR come back, a late camera gets the picture, the checks), `shards_fx` (`intensity` in the material and the description, left out when 1, refused below 0) and on the GPU (`#[ignore]`): a cube six times past white blooms under an HDR camera, a plain one alone spills nothing.
- A symbol for an attack that cannot be blocked (`shards_action::draw`; the manual's "Telegraphs on the ground"): `◌` (U+25CC) is drawn as a vector glyph, a ring with a gap at the top (one stroke of 21 points, radius 0.42 of the glyph's cell, the gap a sixth of the circle), like `!`, `⚠`, `↯`, `✖`, `▲`, `◆`, `↓` and `↑`, in a telegraph's symbol on the ground; `ascii_symbol` gives it the stand-in `C` for the off-screen marker when `ThreatHud::font` is `None`. No existing symbol changes (an unknown one is still a bang), the glyph is presentation only, and no reference replay moves. Tests: `draw.rs` (the ring's strokes, the symbol as one strip, flat in the view plane, the stand-in, a telegraph that names it) and `tests/combat/visuals.rs` (an unblockable action: the ground and the off-screen indicator), on the new test content `ring.action.ron`.
- Held clip phases (the manual's "Phases on the clip (D1.4)"): `ClipDef::holds` names phases of a clip's `phases` whose stretch plays at the authored speed and then holds its last pose for as long as the action's phase lasts, instead of being stretched over it. A long death or downed pose (a 1.2 s fall on a 20 s pose) now falls at its own speed and lies still, where it fell in slow motion; an action's phase shorter than the clip's ends where it ends, and time a run gains past its phases holds a held last phase too. Left out when serialized, so sets without it keep their hashes. `phase_seek` and `phase_place` take the held phases after `loops` (a breaking change of the two functions' signatures). The content check refuses a held phase the clip lacks (or a clip with no `phases`) and a phase in both `loops` and `holds`; the `animation` tool says `by phase, holding down`. Picture only: no replay, snapshot or content hash of a simulation kind changes. The sandbox's downed pose (`spar_down`, 20 s) shows the new `downed` clip (the humanoid's `death`, held). Tests: `anim.rs` (2 new, the phase-check test extended), the sandbox's `animation.rs` (`a_downed_body_falls_at_the_clips_speed_and_then_lies_still`).
- Live tags on attack options (N8d, the boss scheduler; the manual's "Live tags (N8d)" in "Scheduling attacks (bosses)"): `AttackOption::forbid_while` and `solo_forbid_while` (tag lists) and `AttackPlanDef::zone_margin` (meters), all off by default and left out when serialized. An option is not chosen while one of its `forbid_while` tags is live (`Refusal::Live`), nor while one of its `solo_forbid_while` tags is live and one player stands (`Refusal::LiveSolo`); both take their places after `Players` in the rule order, so the director's grant, the planner and `npc_schedule` judge them alike. The live tags (the system parameter `shards_npc::LiveZones`) are the tags of the NPC's own combat objects anywhere (a zone or projectile with a hit window to come or open, a standing summon), of any owner's zone that a body of `zone_margin` meters at the target touches, and of what the other NPCs' attacks in flight on the target lay at its feet (their action's `Spawn` effects with `aim: AtTarget`, one level deep): a capture is live from its caster's press, and of two casters deciding on one tick only the first casts. They are read only for a plan that forbids one, so content without the fields reads what it read before. `npc_schedule` takes `live=[...]` as a what-if and shows `live`; `--check-content` checks the two lists for typos (against the actions' tags only: a plan option's tag is never live) and empty tags, and the margin. Tests: `def.rs` (1), `planner.rs` (1; the rule table now over fourteen rules), `tests/npc/live.rs` (5, the design's run 8, test content `live.*.ron`); `boss_schedule.scenario` lays a chilling puddle at the player's feet and the warden's `heavy` (`forbid_while: ["capture"]`) is refused until its fade; `boss_schedule.json` re-recorded.
- The map widget (U3b; the manual's "UI (E9)", "Map"): `WidgetDef::Map { bind, area, image, width, height, label }` draws a picture over a stretch of the world (`area`: `min` the north-west corner, `max` the south-east, world `x` and `z`; north up) with the places of a game's source (`UiValue::Map { points, you }`, `MapPoint { label, at, state, category }`, `MapState::{Rumor, Discovered, Completed}`; module `shards_ui::map`) and the viewer, which the widget adds. A rumour is drawn on the edge toward the place, a direction rather than a position; a place outside the area sits on the nearest edge. A mark on an edge is drawn whole, just inside it (review). `ThemeDef::map_colors` colours the marks by state and `you`; the theme's `symbols` draw categories. Agents and screen readers read the places in words (a rumour as a compass direction from the viewer). Which places are known, and their states, stay in the game's save. The content check names a bad area (one too large to measure too), size, `bind`, picture (one outside the asset folder too; the `Image` widget's as well) and `map_colors`. A rumour's mark and its words go the same way, from the viewer even off the map; with no viewer to read, the map keeps the one it showed (review).
- Compass categories (U3a; the manual's "UI (E9)", "Compass categories"): the component `ai_core::LandmarkCategory` (it brings `AiLandmark`; like it, it is identity: left out of the replay check and `inspect`, kept by snapshots and sent to co-op clients) gives a landmark a category, from a point of interest's new `PoiDef::category` (skipped when serialized, so existing content is unchanged), a level node's `LandmarkCategory` property or a prefab. `Mark` gains `category`; the compass source `you.heading` keeps the 4 nearest landmarks of each category instead of 4 in all (`you.heading:<n>` for another number; landmarks without a category are one group, so a game without categories sees no change). `ThemeDef::symbols` (`{"shrine": "⛩"}`, spans allowed) draws a category before the name, and screen readers and `ui_read` get its name. The new source `you.nearest:<category>` and `shards::nearest_landmark` give the distance to the nearest landmark of a category. The content check names bad category and symbol names, empty symbols, a symbol's missing span font and a symbol tag that pairs with none.
- Subtitles (U4; the manual's "Audio (E9.5)", "Subtitles"): `SoundDef` gains `subtitle` and `speaker` (text or a message `@id`; each left out of the serialized form when absent, so existing content is unchanged). When the mixer starts such a sound, its line shows for the sound's length plus 0.75 s, at least 2 s, on the voice's clock (a pause holds it); the same line twice shows once, as the newest line, while either voice has time left, and a voice taken by a stronger sound takes its line once no other voice says it. Each voice keeps its own deadline and clock (review): a taken voice takes its time with it, and a `Ui` voice and a world voice saying one line each run out on their own clock. The UI source `subtitles` (`subtitles:3` for three lines, two by default) gives the newest lines, `Speaker: text`, resolved in the language played when read; the new setting `accessibility.subtitles` (on by default) makes it empty. Each line is journaled as it starts (`[local] subtitle: Warden: The gate holds.`), and `audio` lists the lines showing. The content check names an empty `subtitle`, a `speaker` without one, and a message a language lacks. `shards_audio` now depends on `shards_locale` (which `shards_ui` already did). The sandbox's settings screen has a Subtitles toggle (`settings-subtitles`: 51 English messages, 50 Russian).
- Font spans (U1; the manual's "UI (E9)", "Font spans"): `ThemeDef::fonts` names more fonts (`{"symbols": "fonts/Runes.ttf"}`), and `[font=name]…[/font]` in the value of a `Text` or `Hint` widget draws that run in one, as `TextSpan` children at the widget's size and colour, from the screen, a message or a source, drawn again in place when it changes (`shards::ui::markup`: `spans`, `plain`, `fonts_named`). Tags nest; an unclosed tag, a stray `[/font]` and a bad name are text. Screen readers, `ui_read` and `observe` get the text without the tags. The content check refuses a bad font name, a missing font file and a span in a screen's `Text` or `Hint` that names a font the theme `default` lacks; at draw time such a run is in the theme's font, journaled once (`Warn`). Tests: `markup.rs` unit tests (4), `tests/ui/fonts.rs` (2: runs in a named font drawn again in place with a font the theme lacks and the text agents read, the content check). Review: a `Text` or `Hint` whose value stops being text (`UiValue::None`, another kind) drops the runs of the last one, as a value changes and as the widget is drawn again.
- Full-screen flashes (U2; the manual's "UI (E9)", "Full-screen flashes"): the content kind `flash` (`*.flash.ron`: `FlashDef { color, alpha, fade_in_s, hold_s, fade_out_s, pulses, pulse_rate, pulse_depth }`, not a simulation kind) puts a colour over the whole picture for a moment, a reality flash or a glitch's pulses. `shards::ui::flash(world, id)`, the event `PlayFlash` and the dev control tool `flash_play flash=` start one; a newer one replaces the one playing (`ScreenFlash`). It is one full-screen node above the HUD and under menus (`GlobalZIndex` 5, no clicks), on real time, so a hit stop does not hold it. `accessibility.flashes` shapes it on every frame: `reduced` at most 0.35 strong, pulses at most 2 a second and half as deep, eased fades of at least 0.15 s; `off` draws nothing, and the flash is still started and journaled (`[local] flash: …`) for a game's sound. `color` is `#rrggbb` or `#rgb`; the content check refuses `#rrggbbaa` (the strength is `alpha`), a flash of 0 s or over 30 s, a `pulse_rate` of 0 or less when `pulses` is above 0, a depth or alpha outside 0..1. `ai/status` names the flash playing (`ui.flash`). The sandbox has `ui/flashes.flash.ron` (`reality`, `crack`): content 215 definitions in 64 files. Tests: `flash.rs` unit tests (5), `tests/ui/flash.rs` (5: a flash frame by frame, `reduced` and `off`, a newer flash and the trigger, the tool's errors and headless, the content check). Review: the off-screen threat markers moved from `GlobalZIndex` 1000 to 2, over the HUD and under the flash, menus and modals.
- `GltfModel` (`shards_action::render`; the manual's "Animation"): a model from a glTF file loaded whole, put where a `WorldAssetRoot` would go (`GltfModel::load(&asset_server, "models/hero.glb")`, `.scene(i)`). When the file is in it inserts the `WorldAssetRoot` of the scene. Changing the component to another file or scene replaces the scene once that file is in; changed to a scene the file does not have, it takes the old scene away (review: its instance is despawned before the root goes, since removing the root only forgets it). It shares the animation bridge's handle of the file, so the file is read once and the model spawned once: a `file#Scene0` load reads the file again (bevy#10549) and, ending after the bridge's, could spawn the model a second time (a rebind, its props spawned again). The sandbox's and the template's humanoid use it, and so does the sandbox's replay test with animated bodies. Test: `action_render/headless.rs` (1).
- Dev menu entries for the downed state (B5; the manual's "Developer menu and console (E11)"): under combat, `down_me` ("Go down (until revived)", the line `down id=<your body>`) and `down` ("Down a body (bleeds out)...", `down id= bleed=30` finished in the console); the existing `heal` revives. Test: the sandbox's `actions.rs` (1).
- Body parts (R1; the manual's "Body parts (R1)"): one body hit through several volumes. The content kind `parts` (`*.parts.ron`: `PartsDef { parts, body_hurtbox }`, each `PartDef { shape, at, turn, health, damage, to_body, poise, tags, enabled }`, at most 16, checked) and the component `BodyParts` (the prefab check names a missing definition). On the body's first tick the machine that simulates spawns one `AiId` entity per part (`Part`, `PartHealth` when it has health, a kinematic hurtbox-only collider; the body gets `SpawnedParts`), and every tick puts each where `at` says in the body's frame (no bones). A hit that overlaps a part that is on is a hit on the body, once per swing, through the part nearest the middle of the hit (half way up a cone; a projectile's segment start); `body_hurtbox: false` takes hits only through parts. `DamageCtx::part` (`PartHit`) carries the part's factors, already in the hit's damage and poise, and its tags join `ctx.tags`; the body loses `damage * to_body`, the part `damage`; `Damaged::part` names it. A part whose health runs out breaks (`PartBroken { body, part, id, by }`) and stays off. `part_of`, `parts_of`, `set_part`, `break_part`; the dev tool `part id= name= op=on|off|break` (without `id`: the body the caller is locked on); describe `hand of e12 40/60 hp` / ` off` / ` broken`; journal `e5 -> e12 light_1 at hand -10 (hp 1190)`, `e12 hand broken by e5`; the engine rule `action.parts_sane`. A co-op client spawns and despawns no parts: the host's state brings them.
- Sandbox, for R1: the prefab `colossus` (`characters/colossus.prefab.ron`: 6 m tall, 1200 health, no brain), its parts `combat/colossus.parts.ron` (legs that double poise, a hand with 60 health that passes half a hit on, a horn out of reach, a core that starts closed and triples a hit), the locomotion `colossus`, the scenario `parts.scenario` (it spawns the colossus with the yard region, so its closing `regen` takes it away whatever id it had) and the reference replay `tests/replays/parts.json` (nineteen reference replays; `snapshot.json`, `saves.json` and `encounter.json` re-recorded, their saved snapshots now carrying the NPCs' `AimTarget` of FU4b and nothing else new, the fifteen others byte-identical). Content: 213 definitions in 63 files.
- Lock points (R3; the manual's "Lock points (R3)"): a body part with `lock: true` (`PartDef::lock`) is a lock-on target of its own (`LockOnTarget` and the new motion marker `LockPoint` on its entity; a break takes both away), and `PartsDef::lock_body: false` keeps the body itself out of the lock-on (the parts check refuses it with no lock point; the prefab check refuses it with `LockOnTarget` on the prefab). A point may be locked on to while it is not broken and its body stands, is not the locker and is not a fighter of its team; the pick, the switch (`lock_switch`, `lock_on side=`) and the check treat points like bodies, aimed at their centre; a lock on a point is lost with its body (`dead`, `gone`) and let go of when the point breaks. `lock_on point=NAME` (of `target`, else of the body locked on, else of the pick's) and `lock_on target=` on a `lock_body: false` body (the best point it sees); refusals list the points or the parts. Answers, `observe`/`inspect` (a world describer now) and the journal name a point with its body: `lock e17 (hand of e15)`. A swing that overlaps the attacker's locked point goes through it before a nearer part; the camera frames a point at its centre, the legacy pick and the aim assist aim there. What fights is the body (`body_of`, `body_takes_lock`, `lock_points_of`, `lock::target_name`, `lock::pick_point_of`): attack orders keep a lock on a point of their target, finishers, the pair facing, leaps (host and prediction) and `AtTarget` spawns take the body; a projectile aimed or homing at a point flies at its centre and stops homing when the body falls. The `part` tool without `id` acts on the body of a locked point.
- Sandbox, for R3: the colossus's five parts are lock points with `lock_body: false` (its prefab no longer carries `LockOnTarget`); `parts.scenario` locks on to points by name, breaks the locked hand (the lock goes), swings through a locked leg and core, and flicks between points; `parts.json` re-recorded (the eighteen others byte-identical).
- Temporary ground (R2; the manual's "Ground laid in the middle of play (R2)" in "Movement (E2)"): no engine change, a recipe with tests. The controller stands on static and kinematic `WORLD` colliders alike, so a game makes a toppled giant walkable by laying static colliders shaped to its lying pose from the simulation and lifting them when it rises. A collider laid during a tick is ground from the next one. With `AiKind` (which gives it an `AiId`) a snapshot keeps it and co-op sends it to the clients. The navmesh cuts it out at its next refresh (a route planned before that can still cross it) and bakes nothing on top. Tests: `shards_motion` `tests/motion/ground.rs` (3), the sandbox's `snapshots.rs` and `net.rs` (1 each). The foliage section's "what counts as ground" rule is now marked as the scatter's, not the controller's.
- The camera next to a big body (R4; the manual's "The camera next to a big body (R4)"). Measured first: at the defaults a locked camera looks at the player's head, the feet sit at y -0.37 of the frame, and a lock point 6 m up at 2 m ahead, 9 m up at 8 m and 12 m up at any distance reach the top edge or pass it. `FramingTuning::fit: Option<FitTuning>` (`margin` 0.15, `tilt_max` 0.6, `rate` 6; default `None`, no change) turns the view up from the focus (`ThirdPersonCamera::tilt`, `fit_tilt`, measured in the camera's own frame) just far enough to put the target on the top margin, never so far that the feet leave the bottom one; when both cannot be kept, the player is. The turn eases at `rate`, but `fit_room` holds it to the feet and `tilt_max` on every frame. A target the frame already holds gives the very same camera, and the view comes back after the lock. A lower camera is the existing `framing.pitch_clamp`. Content errors for a bad margin, `tilt_max` or rate. The sandbox's `combat/camera.camera.ron` turns the fit on (content: 214 definitions in 64 files). Tests: `camera::math` (1), `tests/motion/camera_big.rs` (6, and the `#[ignore]` measurement), `camera_content.rs` (1).
- A separate aim sensitivity (K1, GDD §21.2; the manual's "Offsets" in "Camera" and "Settings (E8.6)"): `controls.aim_sensitivity` (0.05 to 5, default 1; `ControlSettings::aim_sensitivity()`) beside `mouse_sensitivity`, copied to the new `CameraSettings::aim_sensitivity`. The game says when the player aims with the new `CameraOffsets::aiming` (it owns and resets it, as `fov_add_deg`); while it is set, free look turns by the aiming speed instead of the free one. A lock frames as before; what aiming is stays the game's. Presentation only: no replay changes. Sandbox: an "Aim speed" slider in the settings' controls (`settings-aim-speed`, en and ru; locale: en 51 messages, ru 50). Tests: `camera.rs` (1: the same mouse turns the view by each speed), and the settings copy (`input.rs`), the saved file (`persist.rs`), the listing and the range check (`tools.rs`) extended.
- Camera auto-centre (K2, GDD §21.2; the manual's "Auto-centre (K2)" in "Camera"): `controls.camera_auto_centre` (default off, so no picture changes until the player asks), copied to the new `CameraSettings::auto_centre`. Free look turns back behind the followed body once the look has been left alone for `CameraTuning::auto_centre.delay_s` (1.5 real seconds; `ThirdPersonCamera::look_idle_s`) while the body walks at least `min_speed` (0.5 m/s), at `rate` (2.5/s, exact), and toward `pitch` when set; a body facing more than `max_turn_deg` (150) from the camera's forward does not swing it round. Look input, a lock and a teleport (a jump of the focus, or the body placed farther than `body_snap_distance` in one frame) start the wait over; the walk is measured from where the body is drawn (`ThirdPersonCamera::subject_seen`), not from the focus, which goes on catching up after the body stops. The step is `camera::math::auto_centre`. Content errors for a bad delay, rate, speed, turn limit or pitch. Presentation only: no replay changes. Sandbox: a "Camera auto-centre" toggle in the settings' controls (`settings-auto-centre`, en and ru; locale: en 52 messages, ru 51). Tests: `camera::math` (1), `camera::tuning` (extended), `tests/motion/camera.rs` (3: a short teleport and a stop from a run; walking east, a look that starts the wait over, walking away, toward the camera and standing still, with the setting on and off; a lock framed the same either way), the settings copy (`input.rs`), the saved file and the listing (`tests/settings`) extended.
- Scene cameras (K3, GDD §6.3 and §15.1; the manual's "Scene cameras (K3)" in "Camera"): the content kind `shot` (`*.shot.ron`, `ShotDef { keys, blend_in_s, blend_out_s, skippable }`, presentation). A key is where the camera stands (`eye`) and what it looks at (`look`) at `at` seconds, with an optional field of view, an `ease` (`Linear`, `InOut`) and `cut`; a point is a place (`At`) or a place on a body the game names (`On { role, offset, local }`), where the body is drawn (between ticks, as the orbit camera follows it), and a body that disappears holds its last place. `camera::shot::play(world, id, &roles)`, `stop` (the game's skip; refused for `skippable: false`) and `cancel`; a shot blends in from what the camera showed and back to the orbit camera, which stands still meanwhile (no look, no lock-on pick or switch). `Time<Virtual>`, drawn in `PostUpdate` after `CameraSystems::Orbit`. The events `ShotStarted` and `ShotEnded { skipped }`, `Local` journal lines, an `observe` line, the resource `Shots`, the developer tools `play_shot id=… role.NAME=eN` and `stop_shot [force=true]`.
