Refused with what to fix: a missing or broken shot, a role not given or not the shot's, a body that does not exist, and a headless game (no camera). Content errors for keys out of order or past 120 s, `eye` equal to `look`, a field of view outside 10 to 120, an empty role, a number that is not finite, a blend outside 0 to 10 s, a field a point does not have (`ShotPoint` denies unknown fields). The blend back starts from what the shot showed at its end, so a blend in longer than the shot goes back from where it got to. Presentation only: no replay changes. Tests: `camera::shot` (3: keys, eases, holds and cuts; a role turning its offset; the check), `tests/motion/camera/shot.rs` (5, `render`: a shot moving between its keys and back to the orbit camera, with the look held; a skip blending back from where a followed body was last seen, lock-on picking nobody; the refusals, a cancel, a shot replacing another; a shot shorter than its blend in going back without a jump; a role followed where its body is drawn between ticks), `camera_content.rs` (1: the headless refusal and the file's errors, a misspelt field).
- Rules, slice C3.4a (stamina cost; the manual's "Rules (C3)", "Stamina cost (C3.4a)"): what an action costs is its `cost.stamina` times every `stamina_cost` rule of the body's class that matches one of its `tags` or its id (`shards_action::stamina_cost`). The start check reads it (`Blocker::Stamina` reports the ruled `need`), a start pays it, and a charge's turn pays the difference of the two ruled costs. `FighterView` gains `rules: Option<RulesView>`, filled at every site that judges a start: the machine, an agent's `Order::Attack` (an order presses only what the machine will start), a client's prediction, the NPC director, planner, chains and strike notes, and `FighterView::of` for the tools. New `RulesView::of` and `RulesView::of_entity` in `shards_rules`. A body without a class, a game without rules and an action that costs nothing pay `cost.stamina` exactly. **Breaking:** `FighterView::new` takes the rules as a sixth argument (`None` for none). Tests: `shards_action/tests/combat/rules_costs.rs` (5), `shards_npc/tests/npc/rules.rs` (1, with the test action `dear_jab` and the moveset `spender`), `shards_rules` `read.rs` (1).
- The boss scheduler's plan memory control, slice N8c (the manual's "What a plan remembers is the game's to control (N8c)"): `shards_npc::memory` with `reset_plan(world, entity, why)` (forgets the history and the cooldowns; the attack count, the rest, the grant and any chain stay) and `set_plan_cooldown(world, entity, option, seconds, CooldownSet::{AtLeast, Exactly})` (by option name, `auto` options included; `Exactly` 0 takes the option off cooldown). Both run at once on the world, host only (`HOST_OWNS_PLANS`), and say how to fix a bad call. The events `PlanReset` (`e6 plan reset (encounter wiped): 3 attacks forgotten, 2 cooldowns cleared`, only when there was something to forget) and `PlanCooldownSet` (`e6 heavy cooldown 0.00 s (was 4.20 s)`, only when the value changes) are journaled. The dev tools `npc_plan_reset` and `npc_plan_cooldown` call them; both are in the developer menu's `ai` group. The manual gives the game's reset recipe: an observer of `NpcStateChanged` leaving a `return_home` state, and of `EncounterWiped` and `EncounterReset` for members that do not walk home. Tests: `tests/npc/schedule.rs` (4, and the content-reads test calls the dev tools in a third session), `tests/npc/warden.rs` (1: a game's observer resets the warden home again), the sandbox's `encounter.rs` (2: the yard's pack wiped, walkers reset once on arrival, members placed home at once reset on the wipe), `memory.rs` (1). The scenario `boss_schedule.scenario` sets and clears a cooldown and resets the plan; `boss_schedule.json` is recorded again.
- The boss scheduler's plan audit, slice N8e (the manual's "The audit of a plan (N8e)"): `shards_npc::audit`, pure functions of content. `may_follow(plan, a, b)` judges the plan's own sequence rules (bans, `forbid_after`, `max_repeats: 1`). `follow_pairs(db, plan, moveset, rest_scale)` gives every ordered pair the plan lets follow with the gap between the end of `a`'s danger and `b`'s first hit window, `b` starting after `rest.0 * rest_scale`; an attack runs through its `then` actions once each and its danger includes what its `Spawn` effects lay down (a zone's windows, a projectile's flight until its `lifetime` and its impact; a summon that `stay`s is not counted), and a chain is judged with and without its continuation (the smaller gap kept, `chained`; the cancel ends the attack's own windows, while what it laid down before flies or burns on). Options of weight 0 are left out: the scheduler never chooses them. `plan_hits` lists every hit through chains, `then` and spawned objects, with `times` for a window that repeats and for the objects that run it. `unreachable_options` lists the options the moveset cannot press. The dev query `plan_audit` (`plan`, `moveset`, `min_gap`, `rest_scale`) answers `pairs` shortest first, `hits`, `unreachable` and, with `min_gap`, `short` with a `why`; without `moveset` it names the movesets of the prefabs whose NPC fights by the plan. The engine has no threshold: the verdict is the game's. Tests: `tests/npc/audit.rs` (10, with the test content `audit.*.ron`), the sandbox's `content.rs` (1: the warden's plan).
- The boss scheduler's gates, slice N8a2 (the manual's "Scheduling attacks (bosses)"): an `attack_plan` option gains `angle: Some((min, max))` (degrees off the NPC's facing, 0 ahead, 180 behind; `shards_npc::target_angle`, flat, `ops::atan2`), `players: Some((min, max))` (living players, `schedule::living_players`), `max_repeats: Some(n)` (not while the newest `n` attacks of the NPC's history are all this option) and `forbid_after: [tags]` (not right after an attack carrying one of them, the newest history entry only); all off by default and skipped when serialized, so existing content, replays and hashes are unchanged. `AttackPlanDef::memory()` is the largest of the bans' `within` and the options' `max_repeats`. `Refusal` gains `Angle`, `Players`, `Repeats` and `ForbidAfter` in their places in the order (weight, cooldown, angle, closed, players, repeats, `forbid_after`, ban, machine, budget, overlap, spacing); `Resolved` carries the four fields; `choice_pool` is the pool `choose` draws from. Validation names the field and the fix (`angle`, `players`, `max_repeats`, an empty tag) and checks `forbid_after` tags against every action's tags, every plan option's tags and name and every moveset input (a typo gets a "did you mean"). The query `npc_schedule` (player scope; `target`, `distance`, `angle`, `players` ask "what if") reports which options an NPC may choose now, the first rule that refuses each other option, each eligible option's chance and what the NPC is doing about a choice, built with the planner's own `inputs()` and `refusal()` (public body `schedule_report`, `ScheduleParams`, `WhatIf`); the host owns the answer (a client is refused), an NPC that does not perceive its target is not judged against it without `target=`; a dev caller also gets `pick`, the next tick's draw, for a grant holder on a decision tick. `npc_state` adds `plan.repeats` for a plan with a `max_repeats`. The engine rule `npc.plan_sequence` (with `PlanAudit` and the system `audit_presses`, only with engine invariants on; 28 engine rules) judges every planner press against `max_repeats` and `forbid_after` with its own code, at the press and not at the start. Tests: `shards_npc` `src/` (new: serialization and round trip, `memory()`, validation, the rule table over twelve rules, repeats, `forbid_after`, players and angle windows, `target_angle`, the audit's control test) and `tests/npc/schedule.rs` (repeats over 3000 ticks, angle on a slow-turning body, a phase swap, the press-not-start rule, the reproduced draw over 50 seeds, the report against the planner, the players gate, tool errors, content reads); the scenario `boss_schedule.scenario` and the reference replay `tests/replays/boss_schedule.json` (16 recorded), the stand scenario `stand/boss_schedule.scenario`. Not in this slice: `live` in `npc_schedule` and the live-tag fields (N8d), `tokenless` and the plan's `wait` (N8b), `reset_plan` (N8c), the audit helpers (N8e), the fallback (N8f).
- The boss scheduler's tokenless mode and a plan's own waiting role, slice N8b (the manual's "Scheduling attacks (bosses)"): two `attack_plan` fields, off by default and skipped when serialized, so existing content, replays and hashes are unchanged. `tokenless: true` makes the NPC's grant outside the director's books: it is granted whenever it asks and some option may be chosen (whatever the limit), it is not counted, limited, lowered with the living players or returned `unused` after `patience`, and it triggers no `TokenGranted`/`TokenReturned`; its own choice and press skip the target's `budget` and the `allowed` combinations (`Budget` and `Overlap` never refuse it), while its strikes still space the others (with `ignores_spacing`, it never waits itself) and its attacks in flight still count against the others' budget and combinations. It is read from the plan each time, so no component, `Grant` or `NpcBrain` field is new (a hot reload flips it at once). `wait: Some((role, distance, speed, orbit, period))` gives the NPC a waiting role of its own (validated like a director's `waiting` entry, under `wait.`): it takes no slot and waits in it while it rests and while it holds a grant with nothing in reach (the planner calls `wait_in_role`, on decision ticks and between them); the director's step 4 keeps the role and its orbit while it holds a grant, and a press clears the role. The engine rule `npc.tokens_within_limit` (`audit_tokens`) leaves tokenless grants out of its count. `npc_state` adds `plan.tokenless` and `token.counted: false` (only when set); `npc_schedule` adds `tokenless`, counts the tokens in use without tokenless grants and says `between decisions: waits in its role` and `no grant yet: tokenless, ...`. Tests: `tests/npc/schedule.rs` (the boss among two adds under one spacing director, both adds holding tokens while it attacks, the counted control, an unreachable grant never returned unused, a lowered limit, a hot reload flipping the mode, no slot and a moving orbit with and without a grant, the strafe turning back, a press clearing the role, the budget, a snapshot at every 30th tick, the tool texts) and `def.rs`; the scenario `boss_schedule.scenario` now runs the warden on tokenless plans with a waiting role among two skeletons of one director (the skeletons take tokens, the warden none) and its reference replay `tests/replays/boss_schedule.json` is recorded again; `stand/boss_schedule.scenario` checks the counted half of the token rule in co-op: the host holds the grant of the warden's attack under way and a client sees `token: null` for it (the tokenless half is not reachable there: a co-op session refuses `content_apply` and the sandbox has no tokenless plan; left open).

- The lethal hook and the downed state (B5; the manual's "Death, downed and revive (B5)"). **The hook**: `ActionAppExt::add_lethal_hook(fn(&mut World, &Lethal) -> Fate)` (the resource `LethalHooks`, registered at build time), asked in registration order before every death in play: a hit's (`LethalCause::Hit`), `deal_damage`'s (`Damage`) and `check_deaths`'s (`Health`; `LethalCause` is `non_exhaustive`). `Lethal { entity, killer, source, action, cause }`. The first answer that is not `Fate::Die` wins: `Survive { health, grace }` (health clamped to `(2 * EPS, max]`, a `Grace`, `Survived`; the rest of the hit, its statuses, poise and reaction, goes on) or `Down { bleed }`; with no hook, or hooks that all say `Die`, the death is `kill_with` with the arguments it always had, so the reference replays did not change. For a hit the hooks are asked right after the damage, before the hit's event, so the event's weight follows the answer (a death or a fall is weighed without the break it never makes, a survive keeps it: `the_kill_weight_follows_the_lethal_hooks_answer`); the fate is carried out after the event, not at all if an observer of the event healed the body. `kill` and `kill_with` stay outright; `lethal(world, entity, killer, source, action, cause)` is public. **Down**: `go_down(world, entity, bleed, killer)` puts the lazy `Downed { bleed, by }` on the body, sets its health to 0, drops its buffer and push, ends its statuses (if an `on_expire` hook kills, revives or despawns the body, nothing more follows), triggers `WentDown` and forces the moveset's new `downed` pose (`MovesetDef::downed`; without one the action is cut). A downed body's hits are `Ignored` (`IgnoreReason::Downed`), `deal_damage` and the `Heal` effect pass over it, no reaction or stagger starts, nothing starts (`Blocker::Downed`, `FighterView::downed`), its orders end and the movement tools refuse it as they refuse the dead, the controller does not walk it, and it is no target: `Standing = (Without<Dead>, Without<Downed>)` filters perception, the director's player count and targets, the scheduler's players, the overlay, `lock_on`'s nearest enemy and lock checks, attack orders and the hostile pair selection, and a homing projectile (B4) stops following a downed target; a downed NPC is inactive. `tick_downed` (`ActionSystems::Deaths`, after B4's `tend_objects`, chained before `check_deaths`, `AiId` order) bleeds a body out when its time is up (within half a tick of 0): `bleed_out(world, entity)` kills it, credited to the one who laid it down; `check_deaths` passes over downed bodies. **Revive**: `revive_downed(world, entity, health_share, grace, by)` (health share, the pose cut, poise whole, `Grace`, `Revived`), the action effect `Revive { health, grace }` on the running action's pair victim (checked: a pair that selects `Downed`, `health` in `(0, 1]`, a finite `grace >= 0`), and `PairState::Downed` (`Broken` and `Any` never select a downed body). **Grace**: the lazy `Grace { left }` ignores hits (`IgnoreReason::Grace`) while `left > EPS`; `grant_grace(world, entity, seconds)`; `tick_grace` in `ActionSystems::Fighters` after the body state. **A fallen attacker** (dead or down) cuts its pair, and a victim whose poise is still broken goes back into its stagger for exactly the rest of its break (`resume_stagger`, public; ledger 13a "B3 as built"); a pair whose victim goes down is lost unless it takes downed bodies. The encounter module registers the out rule `downed` (a wipe counts every incapacitated player). A save keeps a downed body as dead; a load into a downed body ends the downed state. Net events `WentDown`, `Revived`, `Survived`; the predictor holds a client's own downed body and snaps on `WentDown` and `Revived`. Describe `downed 14.2s`, `grace 0.80s`; journal `e7 is down (bleeds out in 20.0s)`, `e7 revived by e5`, `e5 survived a lethal hit (hp 1)`; the condition `downed`; the tools `interact` (player, `hold=`) and `down` (dev, `id= bleed=`), and `heal` stands a downed body up. Engine rules `action.downed_sane` and `action.grace_sane` (with control tests; 30 engine rules with B4's two and N8a2's one). Tests: `shards_action/tests/combat/lethal.rs` (28), `shards_npc` (`downed_is_no_target_for_npcs`, `a_downed_player_counts_as_gone`), `shards_encounter` (`a_downed_body_is_out_and_a_wipe_counts_dead_and_down_alike`), the sandbox's `snapshots.rs` (`a_downed_body_and_a_revive_come_back`) and `saves.rs` (`loaded_zero_health_body_stays_dead`).
- Sandbox, for B5: the lethal hook `spare` (`src/gameplay/fate.rs`: a fighter whose moveset has a `downed` pose goes down instead of dying to a hit while another player body stands, and bleeds out when the pose ends), `combat/downed.action.ron` (`spar_down`, 20 s; `spar_revive`: interact held 3 s within 1.6 m of a downed ally, cut at 2 m or on a hit, 30 % health and 1 s of grace; net ids 200-201) and `combat/downed.moveset.ron` (the moveset `partner`: the duelist's kit, the pose and the revive bound to interact); the scenarios `downed.scenario` and `stand/downed.scenario` (a client goes down, a partner revives it, a second fall bleeds out, at 150 ms and 2 % loss), the reference replay `tests/replays/downed.json` (eighteen reference replays with A2e's `voxel_nav.json`; the sixteen older ones, B4's `objects.json` and N8a2's `boss_schedule.json` among them, re-recorded byte-identical). Content: 210 definitions in 61 files (with D1.1's prop).
- B5 with FU4 (where the two meet): a spawn aims only at a standing body (`target_of` passes over a dead or downed lock-on, `AimTarget` or flight target to the next, and with none left skips the spawn as before; `shards_npc`'s drive mirrors only a standing brain target into `AimTarget`); a paired run's aim (FU4) follows its victim only while the pair still holds it (`keeps_victim`, the rule `tend_pairs` keeps a victim by: never a dead one, a downed one only for a revive), so a revive's run turns to its downed ally rather than to a lock-on; a revive lets go (`lost`) of an ally that stood up some other way first (a `heal`, a game's `revive_downed`) instead of holding it `Paired` to the end of its run (CodeRabbit), and keeps the ally its own `Revive` stood up until it ends `finished`; a blow a lethal hook answers `Survive` keeps its statuses, which name the attacker as `StatusApplied.source`, one it answers `Down` builds none up, and a body that falls while a hit's statuses apply takes no more of them; a victim the pair's blow lays down ends `(lost)`, never `(killed)`, and a fallen attacker's pair ends `(cut)`. `PairAngle::Intent` measures from a lock-on only while its target stands.
- Props and clip families (D1.1, the base of the animation lane), all picture only and unserialized at their defaults (content hashes and the reference replays do not change; no component is simulation state). `animset` gains `sockets` (a bone, an offset and a turn), `carry` (what a moveset carries, `"*"` any other), `mounts` (where a carried prop goes: by action key, looked up by id, `anim` and tags, and `"@moveset"` for the seconds after a weapon switch: `moves` with a `Span`, `previous`, `ended_by`, `on_cut`) and `by_moveset` (a clip family: a rename table applied to every clip the bridge picks). The content kind `prop` (`PropDef`: a glTF `model` or a box `size`, `look`, `grip`, `turn`, `scale`; not a simulation kind). `ActionClip::ends_equip` (true for a whole-body mapping, false for a layered one; written only when it differs). The bridge (`render::props`) spawns the props under the socket's bone (`PropOf`, no `AiId`, not reflected), evaluates their place every frame from the action's time and the simulation clock (`mount_places`, `equip_open`, `cut_holds`: pure, in `shards_action::anim`), spawns them again when the model is spawned again or their definition changes, and takes the props, the equip and the cut hold over to a new binding; every walk of the skeleton by bone name skips the subtrees below a prop, and a prop's glTF gets no player. A graph is rebuilt only when `clips`, `layers`, the layered mappings or `by_moveset` change. `PropSwaps` (a body shows a variant of a carried prop; reflected, `replay_ignore`, `net_ignore`), the read-only `Sockets` system parameter (`bone`, `world`), `select_clip_in` and `select_layer_in` (the pure choice for a moveset's family), the dev tool `animation` (`id`, `animset` to preview a set on a body that has no `Animated`, `bones`; answers headless too), the `anim` field of `ai/status` and a developer menu item "world / animation". `shards_action` now depends on `shards_look` (a prop's `look`). The sandbox: sockets on the humanoid, `content/props/sandbox.prop.ron` (a sword), the player carries it in the right hand, the drink puts it on the hip and a cut drink leaves it there for 0.05 s. Content check: moveset, prop, socket, clip, phase and event names, a mount of a prop nobody carries, `@` keys other than `@moveset` (the motion-mode keys come with a later slice), and with an asset folder a socket's bone and a prop's model file. Review fixes: an action's own `previous` span decides over the weapon switch's, the first moveset a body shows is its baseline, `root_motion` rebinds, the status counts a held body, the `animation` tool prints the places the bridge computed (cut holds and previous props), a prop `model` may not leave the assets folder. Tests: `crates/shards/tests/action_render/sockets.rs` (18), `shards_action` `anim::mounting` (17), `tests/combat/golden.rs` (`existing_animsets_keep_their_json`), `sandbox/tests/sandbox/animation.rs`, `scenarios/animation.scenario`, `replays.rs` (`a_replay_verifies_with_animated_bodies_and_the_picture_does_work`: the combat prototype replay played with every fighter dressed and bound, identical). Not done: the later slices of the animation design (motion-mode mounts, model facing, the phase time map, gaits, overlays, feet, remote smoothing, additive layers), and the lead's edits of the ledger and the plan (see the pull request).

- Motion modes (D1.2, the second slice of the animation lane), picture only and left out of a set's JSON at their defaults (content hashes of the sets that do not use them and the reference replays do not change). `mounts` gains the keys `"@mode:<mode>"` (rules while the drawn `MotionState::mode` is `<mode>`: `ground`, `air`, `water`, `glide`, `mantle`; `Secs` counted from when the rules begin to apply; `yield_after` makes them step aside while an action runs in the mode and for that many seconds after it) and `"@left:<mode>"` (rules after the body left the mode: the moves are read at time 0 for `wait` seconds, or until the body's fade out of its clip is done, then run from 0), `MountRules::{yield_after, wait}`, and the presentation event `ModeLeft { entity, mode }` the bridge fires when a `@left` wait ends (the `left:<mode>` overlay event of a later slice). Rules apply in the order: the cut hold, the running action, `@mode`, `@left`, `@moveset`, the carry socket; `@mode` rules that apply end a weapon switch's equip, and a whole-body action ends a pending wait or draw. `LayerDef::off_in` (modes in which nothing plays on a layer; a play fades out over the locomotion clip's blend) and `AnimSet::drops` (`DropDef { down, seconds }`: the model lowered by a swing afloat at a constant rate and raised the same way). The new public stage set `render::AnimSystems { Root, Model }` in `PostUpdate` (after the clips and the render interpolation, before transforms propagate): `hold_root_motion` is `Root`, and `Model` writes the drop onto the `root_motion` bone in its parent's space (metres whatever the model's scale), never onto the interpolated model child. `shards_action::{mode_name, parse_mode, MODES, MODE_KEY, LEFT_KEY}`; `MountView` gains `mode` and `left`. The `animation` tool prints a mode line (the mode, whether the `@mode` rules apply or step aside, a pending left, the layers that are off) and a `model:` line with the drop. The graph shape no longer holds a layer's `off_in`: editing it rebuilds nothing. Content check: unknown mode names (with a hint), `@` keys other than `@moveset`, `@mode:` and `@left:`, `yield_after` outside `@mode:`, `wait` outside `@left:`, `previous` and `on_cut` under a mode key, `drops` without `root_motion`, a drop that is not positive. The sandbox: the `mover` moveset (the body-motion scenario's) carries the sword, which rides its back in the pool and stays in the hand for its stroke, the model sinks 0.15 m for the stroke, and the arms' layer is off in the water. Tests: `crates/shards/tests/action_render/modes.rs`, `render::modes` (the clocks and the drop, pure), `anim::modes` (content), `sandbox/tests/sandbox/animation.rs`. A new binding of the same bones (a rebuilt graph) keeps what the drop wrote on the bone; a missing `root_motion` bone is warned once. Not done: the overlay that plays on `left:<mode>` (D1.6), the rest of the later slices.
- The phase map (D1.4, the animation lane's third slice; the manual's "Phases on the clip (D1.4)"), picture only and left out of a set's JSON at its default (the reference replays do not change). A clip says the phases it was timed to (`ClipDef::phases`, clip seconds, in the actions' form), and an action with the same phase names in the same order is seeked phase by phase instead of evenly: an enemy's long windup shows the clip's windup and strikes as its active phase starts. Time past the phases (a miss's longer recovery) stretches the last phase; `ClipDef::loops` names phases of open length whose stretch repeats at the authored speed. It applies to the body's clip, a layer's, and the stagger and dead clips, each by the clip played (a family clip by its own). New: `phase_seek`, `ActionTime`, `AnimSet::progress`, `select_clip_timed` and `select_layer_timed` (the bridge and the `animation` tool use them; `select_clip_in` and `select_layer_in` still seek evenly). Checks: a clip's phases (a name, once, >= 0 s, some length in all), `loops` naming them, an action that shares some of a clip's phase names but not all in order, and with the asset folder a clip's phases against the file's `phases` extra (half a tick) or its length. The `animation` tool says `by phase` or why a clip plays evenly. Sandbox: the attack, roll, block and drink clips carry their phases, so the warden's and the skeletons' swings strike on time. Tests: `anim.rs` (+6), `bake.rs` (+1), `render/bridge.rs` (+2). Review: a looped phase going round fires the cues of its stretch once more and never those of the phases before it, on the body and on a layer (`phase_place` gives the stretch, `ClipChoice::repeats` carries it, `crossed_cues_in` takes going back inside it as a wrap; before, it was a restart that fired every earlier cue again); the check also pairs a moveset's `stagger` and `death` actions that map no clip of their own with the set's `stagger` and `dead` clips (in the moveset's family), which they are seeked on; and the `animation` tool seeks ahead by the fixed clock's overstep for an action without a hold loop, as the bridge does, so it reports the seek that is drawn (test in the sandbox's `animation.rs`).
- Facing (D1.3, the animation lane's fourth slice, with D1.5's remnant; the manual's "Facing (D1.3)"), picture only and left out of a set's JSON at its default (the reference replays do not change). A body that faces a target (`LocomotionInput::face`) shows which way it moves from its facing: `Heading` (forward, back, left, right; quarters with 10° of slack, `HEADING_SLACK`) goes into the new `BodyStatus::facing`, and `locomotion` takes a gait's heading keys (`walk.back`, `run.left`, `sprint.right`, ...) and `idle.facing` (`FACING_KEYS`, `facing_key`, `AnimSet::facing_clip`). With none authored, `back` plays the gait's clip backward and `left`/`right` the clip as it is; a backward clip fires its cues going down (`crossed_cues_back`). A switch between gaits' clips carries the stride (`ClipChoice::gait`, `carry_phase`). `ActionClip::turn` (seconds): a run of a `face: Keep` action turns the model to its heading and back, written on the `root_motion` bone in `AnimSystems::Model` (`render/turn.rs`; `AnimationBound::model_turn`, `heading`). `ActionClip` is no longer `Eq`. Checks: the new locomotion keys, `turn` >= 0, whole body only, with `root_motion`, and not on a mapping a `face: Run` action plays. The `animation` tool says the heading, a clip played backward and the model's turn. Sandbox: `spar_roll_keep` shows the roll clip and turns the model (`turn: 0.08`). Tests: `anim.rs` (+5), `render/turn.rs` (+4), `render/bridge.rs` (+4). Review: a walk that turns round on the same clip (a lock-on let go, a heading with no strafe clip) fires only the cues it passed: Bevy moved the clip at the last frame's speed, so the bridge reads its cues the way set then (`AnimationBound` keeps it); before, each turn fired a burst of almost every cue.
- Event overlays (D1.6, the animation lane's fifth slice; the manual's "Event overlays (D1.6)"), picture only and left out of a set's JSON at their defaults (the reference replays do not change). `AnimSet::overlays` maps an engine event (`OVERLAY_EVENTS`: `blocked`, `parried`, `damaged`, `guard_broken`, `staggered`, `part_broken`, `landed`) to `OverlayDef { clip, layer, fade_in, fade_out: (start, end), strength }`: the clip plays over the layer from the event on the simulation's clock, its share rising to `strength` over `fade_in`, falling along a smoothstep between `fade_out`'s two times and ending there (`OverlayDef::share`, `ended`); the action's clip and locomotion keep what it leaves of the layer. The same event again starts over from the share it has; another event's overlay takes over; death, a layer going off in the motion mode and an overlay removed from the set cut it over the locomotion blend. The overlay clips get masked nodes of their own (`Built::overlay_node`); observers on the seven events note them on the drawn body (`AnimationBound::overlay_on`, `OverlayPlay`); the clip's cues fire, through the bones as a body or layer cue does (a cue at a bone carries its place and strength, D1.7). `ActionClip::blend_out` (and `LayerChoice::blend_out`): how long a layered mapping's clip takes to leave its layer. Checks: the event (with a "did you mean"), clip, layer, `fade_in` >= 0, `0 <= fade_out.0 <= fade_out.1` and `fade_out.1 > 0`, `strength` in (0, 1], `blend_out` >= 0 and only on a layered mapping. The `animation` tool lists the overlays and the one playing on each layer, and `event=` starts one on a drawn body; `AnimStats::overlays_started`. Sandbox: the humanoid flinches on its upper body when damaged (`strength: 0.6`) and when it blocks (`0.35`). Tests: `anim.rs` (+2), `render/bridge.rs` (+6), the sandbox's `animation.rs` (+1). Review: a hit the body ignored (`Damaged::invulnerable`) starts no `damaged` overlay; a layered clip taken over leaves no faster than the new one comes in (a short `blend_out` let the walk through); `event=` needs `id=` even with one body drawn, and a dead body is said to play nothing; a clip taken over in the middle of its own ramp (two takeovers in a row) keeps the share it showed instead of a fraction of it, so the layer does not drop to the walk as the third clip starts.

- Cues at a bone (D1.7, a slice of the animation lane), picture and sound only and left out of a set's JSON at their defaults (the content hash of a set that uses neither field and the reference replays do not change). `CueDef` gains `bone` (a node of the model, e.g. `foot.L`) and `strength` (0 or more, 1 by default). `AnimationCue` gains `bone`, `at` (the bone's world position when the cue fires, from its `GlobalTransform` as of the last drawn frame, found by node name under the model and never below a prop; `None` without a bone or when the model lacks it, which the bridge says once in the log) and `strength`, plus `AnimationCue::new` and `CueDef::new` for the old shape. The fx module plays a cue's effect at `EffectAt::Point(at)` instead of the body's feet and scales the burst and the particles' size (and what an effect with a `rate` emits later, over the same `duration`) by the strength: `shards_fx::play_scaled` and `play_quietly_scaled` (`play` and `play_quietly` call them at 1; the rounded count is never below one while the effect has particles; strength 0 plays nothing and leaves the pools alone), and `Emitter` gets `strength`. The audio module plays the sound at `SoundAt::Point { position: at, source: Some(entity) }` (it stays where it fell) with the strength as a linear gain on the sound's volume: `shards_audio::play_scaled` (`play` calls it at 1; a gain of 0 starts no voice). The content check refuses an empty `bone` and a `strength` that is negative or not a number, and with the asset folder a `bone` that is no node of the model's glTF file (the set's `source`, also for a clip from another file: review). The sandbox's `walk` and `run` footsteps name `foot.L` and `foot.R`.

- Hit stop: a rule can be held to solo play. `When::solo` (off by default and left out of the file at its default) matches only a machine that is not in a co-op session (`NetRole::Single`; a host counts as co-op even before anyone has joined, and so does a client), because a picture hold in co-op is still a hold: a perfect dodge that the design gives a slow beat to solo players only can say so, and a second rule after it can give co-op a shorter one. `HitInfo` gets the field `solo` (set when the table is consulted, like `local`; a game that builds a `HitInfo` by hand adds `solo: false`). The `hitstop` tool and the journal describe the condition as `solo play only`; the content check finds a rule that an earlier one covers as before. The sandbox's perfect dodge uses it. Tests: `shards_hitstop` (`def.rs`, `tests/hitstop/coop.rs`). `hitstop_play` says so when the only rule for an event is held to solo play and the machine is in co-op (`a rule gives a hold for perfect_evaded, but only in solo play, …`), instead of "no rule of the table gives a hold".
- An order that makes no progress ends (`shards_motion::orders`): a `move` or `move_to` order whose body has not come closer to its goal by `STALL_PROGRESS` (0.1 m) for `STALL_SECONDS` (2 s) ends as `blocked` (a wall, a rooted body), instead of running for ever (a sprint order drained the stamina against a wall). The event `OrderEnded { entity, order, reason }`, the journal line `e1 move_to (20.0, 0.0) ended: blocked (no closer than 0.10 m in 2.0 s)`, and the lazy component `OrderProgress` (which `observe` shows as `last order ended: …` until the next order starts; snapshots keep it, replay checks ignore it) say so; the orders behind it run on. The count waits while an action holds the body or something pushes it, restarts at every 0.1 m gained, and for an order to an entity a body that moves while its target moves too counts (a chase of a runner; a body that circles a target that stands still does not), so a slow walk, a guard, a stagger and a walk round a corner are never ended. When several bodies stall on the same tick their journal lines come in `AiId` order. It runs in the simulation, not in a client's prediction. Tests: `shards_motion/tests/motion/orders.rs`.
- Context and paired actions (B3), all content that is off by default and unserialized at its default (content hashes and the reference replays of older content do not change, and a fighter that uses none of it carries none of the new components). **Memories**: `MovesetDef::remember: [(key, on, seconds, forget)]` (`on`: `Parried`, `PerfectEvaded`, `Blocked`, `Evaded`, `Landed`, `Hurt`, `Ended(tag)`, `Started(tag)`; `forget`: `Hurt`, `Reacted`, `Started(tag)`, `Used`), kept in the lazy component `Memory`; `remember`, `forget`. **Bindings**: `MovesetDef::context: [(input, action, when)]`, resolved each tick in the new first set `ActionSystems::Context` into the lazy `ContextInputs` (per input the first binding whose `when` holds: `Remembers(key)`, `Pair`, `StaminaAbove(v)`, `Condition(name)` from `add_context_condition`); a bound input starts its action from idle, the buffer, a held input or a cancel window, where it beats a `Combo` link or an action override; a binding never holds for the running action; the fixed order is the content order (the sandbox: finisher, counter, cut out of a roll), with no per-binding priority field. **The guard's fresh press**: `GuardDef::perfect_needs_press: (gap: 0.35, entry: 0.15, rearm: true)`: a press at least `gap` after the last one arms the guard; the first guard started within `entry` of it keeps its perfect window, every other start only blocks, and a parry re-arms (the lazy `GuardArm`, the single gate `gate_perfect` in the machine, `force_action` and the predictor). **Paired actions**: `ActionDef::pair: (select: (team, state, range, height, angle, line_of_sight, prefer_target, slack), victim_action, only_victim, exclusive, hold_break, ends_break, break_range, cancel_on_hurt, strike_reaction)`: `start_pairs` takes the victim (the binding's, else the nearest that qualifies, the lock-on target first; `slack` gives it to the nearest player), the lazy `Pairing` and `Paired`, the forced `victim_action`, a held break; the strike ends the break once and forces `strike_reaction` (a knockdown) as `Reaction::forced` with weight `Finisher`; `tend_pairs` ends a pair `Cut` or `Lost` (death stops the run's lunge with the lazy `RunHalted`, `break_range` cuts it), `cancel_on_hurt`; every end through `release_pair` and `PairEnded { end: Finished | Lost | Cut }` after `PairStarted` (both net events); `cut_action`, `hold_break`, `pair_victim`. `ActionDef::weight` is the floor of its hits' `Damaged::weight` (`Counter`). Tools and AI: describers (`remembers parried 0.32s`, `light -> spar_finisher on e7`, `pairing e7`, `held by e5`, `guard press armed`, and the world describer `finishable by e5`, derived, not a component), journal lines `e5 takes e7 with spar_finisher` and `e5 lets go of e7 (finished)`, the conditions `pair_ready` and `paired`, the dev tool `remember id= key= seconds=`, `stagger poise=true`, the HUD source `you.finisher`; the tools answer from a fresh `resolve` (a client from its replicated copy). `ai_core`: `register_ai_world_describer`. Engine rules `action.pairs_match` and `action.memory_sane` (twenty engine rules on by default now, with B2c's). Co-op: the host decides context, the press and the victim on its own tick; a client predicts the bound action from its copy of `ContextInputs`, but not an action with `pair` (13a "Context staleness": with the finisher predicted the stand counted 10 of 10 presses near the end of the stagger mispredicted, so the host starts a finisher and the client takes it over; a press made before the client's copy shows the finisher is a predicted jab the host replaces, 18 of 19 trials in that window; two players at the slack, 12 trials, one finisher each time). A client runs the guard's press rule on its own presses (`step_arm`), so its shown perfect window matches the host's tick by tick. `DamageCtx::pair` tells a damage hook a hit is a pair's strike. Content check: a memory's tag (`Ended`, `Started`, `Forget::Started`) that no action carries is an error. So is a paired action with a `charge` (the charged action would run on with a pair that names the old one). The angle of a pair's `select` is measured from the body's facing. A finisher that kills its victim hits nobody else (`RunHalted` keeps `only_victim`). `strike_reaction: 0` asks for no reaction. The `remember` tool takes at most 600 seconds. Not reproduced: Godot's press stamp taken at the guard's start. Not built: a `Downed` pair state (B5), a pair `select` that skips an intangible (burrowed) victim (B2c's `intangible` makes hits skip it, so a strike on one finds nothing), Godot's guard press cancelling a queued attack. See "Context and paired actions (B3)" in the manual. Tests: `shards_action/tests/combat/context.rs` (28), `def.rs` (`context_fields_keep_old_json`, `context_content_is_checked`), `engine_rules.rs`, `snapshots.rs` (`mid_pair_and_with_a_guard_press_armed`), `net_predict.rs` (`client_finishers_are_confirmed_by_the_host`, `context_staleness_around_the_break_is_counted`, `the_slack_handoff_between_two_players_is_counted`, `a_predicted_guard_shows_the_hosts_perfect_window`, `a_client_parries_and_counters`), `state_audit.rs`.
- Sandbox, for B3: `combat/context.action.ron` (`spar_finisher`, `spar_finished`, `spar_counter`, `spar_dodge_cut`, `spar_dodge`, `spar_parry_guard`, net ids 150-155) and `combat/context.moveset.ron` (the moveset `contextual`); the scenarios `context.scenario` and `stand/finisher.scenario` (two players, the slack, a client's finisher and a client's parry and counter), the reference replay `tests/replays/context.json`. The nine older reference replays were recorded again and came out byte-identical. `npc_duel.scenario` waits 60 ticks after its pack stands down, so the swings in flight end and their attack tokens come back before the next scenario (in one shared game, `npc_navmesh.scenario`'s guard otherwise waited for them).
- Spawned combat objects (B4), all content that is off by default and unserialized at its default (content hashes and the reference replays of older content do not change). `shards_action::objects`: the action effect `Spawn(SpawnDef)` (`prefab` and `action` by index, the last repeating; `at`, `aim: Facing | Target(height) | AtTarget`, `count`, `pattern: Fan(spread) | Line(start, step) | Radial(lines, start, step)`, `delay`, `random_turn` from `SimRng`, `ground`, `stay`), `spawn_objects` (a game's own origin too), `objects_of`, `despawn_objects_of`, `live_tags_at`; the components `CombatObject { owner, spawned, start_in, action, stay }`, `Projectile { speed, lifetime, homing, aim_height, pierce }` (a prefab's), `ProjectileFlight { dir, age, target, from }`, `Repeats`; the event `ObjectsSpawned` (journal `e8 spawns 3 x bolt_fly`). Objects act from the tick after their spawn (`ActionSystems::Objects`, between the machine and the effects); projectiles fly after the controller (homing by `homing * dt`, a wall stops them), their sphere windows sweep the segment flown in the tick, one that does not pierce meets the first target along it whatever the outcome, and `ActionDef::impact` is what it runs where it stops. `HitDef::repeat` (a zone's per-target pulse, 0 or at least 0.05 s), `HitDef::max_targets` (a sweep's targets in one tick, lowest `AiId` first), `ActionDef::then` (the action that starts when this one finishes, on fighters and objects), `CombatTuning::max_objects` (256) and `max_objects_per_owner` (96) with the journal warning `spawn capped`; the content check refuses unknown `then`, `impact`, spawn prefabs and actions, and a circle of `Spawn` and `impact` edges (a `then` loop passes). Credit goes to the owner (`DamageCtx::credit`, events name the object as `source`), an object never hits its owner, a dead or gone owner spawns nothing, and its objects go and deal nothing. `HitZone::owner` and `HitZone::tags`, `hit_zones` lists the objects' zones, `zones_threatening` leaves out a body's own objects; `action_zones` is exported. `shards_content::PrefabCache` and `spawn_prefab_cached`; `ai_core::NoTombstone` (combat objects leave no tombstone); `Local` derives `Reflect`. The describers `object` and `projectile`, the conditions `objects_live` and `projectile_flying`, the query `combat_objects`, the engine rules `action.objects_sane` and `action.objects_capped`; `action.running_sane` covers the objects. The umbrella crate tags a combat object spawned in a region with `RegionPart`. Not done (the game's): which boss casts what and when, spot searches for summons, a ward while a summon lives.
- Sandbox, for B4: `combat/objects.prefab.ron` (`bolt`, `bolt_homing`, `zone`), `combat/objects.action.ron` (`caster_volley`, `bolt_fly`, `bolt_burst`, `chill_puddle`, `caster_line`, `line_zone_0`-`line_zone_5`, `caster_radial`, `ring_zone_0`-`ring_zone_3`, net ids 170-185) and `combat/caster.moveset.ron` (the moveset `caster`); the scenarios `objects.scenario` and `stand/objects.scenario` (two clients have the host's bolts by id and see its hits), the reference replay `tests/replays/objects.json` (it also passes `--heavy 10`). The fourteen older reference replays were recorded again and came out byte-identical. 206 definitions in 58 files.
- B4 review fixes: a prefab `Projectile` with a negative or non-finite number is refused at the spawn, by name; a `Spawn` of more objects than the caps allow, and a non-sphere hit shape in the flight action of a projectile prefab, are content errors; a spawn places only the indices that fit; a non-piercing flight meets one target over all its hit windows; two `random_turn` spawns of one spawner in one tick turn differently; a delayed projectile flies from the tick after it starts; a projectile spawned behind a wall stops at it; an aimed spawn with no target journals why; `action.objects_capped` looks only at ticks that spawned objects (a cap lowered by a reload halts nothing); `NoTombstone` is reflected (no net or snapshot warning). Not predicted: a fighter's `then` in co-op.
- The source of a status buildup (FU4, game ask M4-E2): `StatusApplied.source: Option<AiId>` (the attacker of a hit, the credited owner for a combat object), the new `add_status_buildup_from(world, entity, status, amount, source)` (`add_status_buildup` is unchanged and passes none) and the resource `StatusSource`, which an `on_apply` hook reads. No replay changes. A hook that builds up another status leaves `StatusSource` as it found it (the outer value is restored).
- The target of a brain-driven NPC for spawned objects (FU4, game ask M4-E8): the component `AimTarget(Option<AiId>)`; a `Spawn` with `aim: Target` or `AtTarget` reads `Intent::target`, then `AimTarget`, then a flight's target, and `shards_npc` mirrors the brain's target into it every tick (`Npc` requires it; `replay_ignore`d, so no replay changes). The brain still never writes `Intent.target`.
- A binding the medium refuses does not bind (FU4, game ask M3-E12): a context binding whose action the moveset's `only_in` refuses in the body's motion mode is left out of `ContextInputs` (and ignored by `can_start`), so the input falls to the next binding or the moveset's action, and the HUD's finisher prompt (`you.finisher`) and `finishable by` agree. Only a moveset that has both `context` and `only_in` is affected.
- A paired action aims at its victim, lock or not (FU4, game ask M3-E10): while a pair run is active (at its start and while it runs) the machine and the client's prediction take the pair's victim in place of the lock-on target for the tracking, the assist and the sight check, so a lock on another foe no longer turns the run away and spends the break on a miss. A run started from a moveset input, or from a binding without `When::Pair`, has no victim when the machine aims (the pair's `select` finds one in the same tick, after the start), so that tick the run is turned to the victim it takes, unless the press named a direction or the action has `turn` spans. No new content and no opt-in: content that runs a pair action with a lock on another foe now turns to the victim (the old turn to the lock was the bug); no reference replay does that.
- The angle origin of a pair's candidate (FU4, game ask M3-E11): `PairSelect::angle_from: Facing | Intent` (`PairAngle`). `Facing` (the default, unserialized, so content hashes and replays do not change) is the old rule, the body's facing; `Intent` measures from the lock-on direction first, then the movement input's direction, then the facing.
- The journal word for a pair victim that dies of the pair's own blow (FU4, game ask M3-E13): `lets go of e7 (killed)` (it said `(lost)`). `PairEnded` has a new field `killed` (the victim's `Died` named the attacker and the pair's action) and `describe()`; `end` stays `Lost`, so a game that matches on it is not affected; a victim lost otherwise still says `(lost)`. The short-lived component `PairKilled` carries the fact from the kill to the pair's end (left out of replays). The damage journal line of a killing blow keeps the nominal damage (`-140 (hp 0)` on 122 hp): it is `Damaged.amount`, the hit's damage as dealt (overkill), which games read, the line has no field for the health before the blow, and `hits.rs` pins the text, so it stays.
- Encounters (`shards_encounter`, G9): groups of enemies that come in waves at an anchor, start when the players arrive, end when the
  last counted member falls, rewind when the group of players is wiped, and reset when they leave. A new crate with two content
  kinds, `encounter` (`EncounterDef`: `radius`, `start` as `Trigger`, `Near` or `Manual`, `waves` with `delay`, `placed`, a `warn` cue and
  groups with `count`/`up_to`, `at` as `Offsets`, `Marker`, `Poi` or `Ring`, `face`, `home`, `leash`, `avoid_behind` and JSON-patch
  `overrides`; `tags`, `budget`, `allowed`, `forbidden`, `rules`, `resume_after`, `reset_after`, `on_reset`, `pull_back`, `lock`, `once`,
  `net_always`, `blocks_join`; every default is 0, none or off but `pull_back.heal`, which is on) and `encounter_rules` (`limits` with `unless`, `needs`; not simulation
  content). Two components: `EncounterAnchor` (a prefab, a tile piece, a level node or `place_encounter` puts an encounter anywhere;
  `shards_world` does not change) and `EncounterRole` (a prefab's cost and tags for the checks). Content checks name the file, the
  encounter, the wave, the group and the fix (budget, tags, limits, needs, overrides read like a prefab spawn, `pull_back.state` in each
  member's behaviour and a `return_home` state there, cue effects, sounds and messages, an anchor's encounter and key on every prefab). The state machine
  (`Idle`, `Armed`, `Running`, `Between`, `Wiped`, `Resetting`, `Won`) is a pure function with its table in the docs; every timer is a count of whole
  ticks set and counted down in one tick (1.2 s is 72 ticks, 2.5 s 150, 2.0 s 120), and one transition happens per tick. Counting is level
  based (a member is out when it is `Dead` or gone, so every death counts); a wipe despawns the wave silently and rewinds one wave (not
  between waves), pulls back the survivors of a placed wave (`pulled_back` decides what the resume does, not the wave number), the resume
  comes `resume_after` after someone stands; an absence reset runs in every fighting state. A placed pack that is out, hurt or hunting
  before its start is pulled into the fight; a placed pack whose members are all out clears and is won instead of waiting for ever. Spawn
  points are grounded, checked against the navmesh where the region has one, and kept out of the rear cone of a participant's facing
  (lock-on target, aim, forward: simulation state, never the camera); randomness is a `DetRng` keyed by the seed, tick, run key, wave,
  group and purpose. Events `EncounterStarted`, `WaveWarned`, `WaveStarted`, `WaveCleared`, `EncounterCompleted`, `EncounterWiped`,
  `EncounterResumed`, `EncounterReset`; tools `encounters`, `encounter`, `encounter_start`, `encounter_clear`, `encounter_reset`; conditions
  `encounter_active`, `encounter_between`, `encounter_wiped`, `encounter_won` and the NPC condition `encounter_returning`; the engine
  rules `encounter.members_tracked`, `encounter.state_sane` and `encounter.lock_matches`; the dev menu entries; the snapshot resource
  and replay check `encounters` (absent without runs, so other snapshots and replays keep their bytes) and a snapshot prepare that
  removes stale runs (a co-op host keeps the resource, empty, once it had one, because a removal does not replicate); the world save section `encounters` (the keys of won `once` encounters; a fight in progress is not saved).
  `EncounterAppExt::add_encounter_out_rule` (a downed state counts as out) and `add_encounter_despawn_hook` (what a member owns is
  cleaned up before a silent despawn); `adopt`, `start_encounter` (`wave` 1 of a placed encounter is the plain start, 2 and later skip the placed wave), `reset_encounter`, `place_encounter`, `join_blocked`. The umbrella
  sends the events to clients, plays a warning's effect and sound, keeps `NetAlways` on the members of a `net_always` fight (and takes it off a corpse or released add the run let go), and holds
  joiners while a `blocks_join` fight is on. Module `encounter` (out with `npc` or `world`). A placed pack that has begun once does not begin again for a casualty after a reset; a resume cue leads the spawn by `resume_after`; `count` and `up_to` are at most 64; `Face::Yaw(d)` is the anchor's yaw plus `d`. Manual: "Encounters (G9)".
- `NetHooks::join_gates`: a list of gates (`fn(&World, &JoinRequest) -> Option<String>`) that the new default `join_policy`
  (`default_join_policy`) consults; a gate's reason makes a newcomer wait. A request whose player already has a body (a rejoin within the
  grace) skips them. `gates_say` for a game with a policy of its own.

- Sandbox: the yard (`regen region=yard seed=1`), a 3 x 1 region of 32 m tiles east of the arena (`yard.region.ron`, `yard.tile.ron`, `yard.prefab.ron`;
  new tile bakes in `assets/nav/`): a ring tile with the trigger volume `yard_ring` and the anchor of `ring_waves` (three waves of 1, 2
  and 1 skeletons, a special arena, `once`) and a pack tile with three `pack` markers and the anchor of `yard_pack` (a placed pack of 2 to 3,
  never behind a player); `content/encounter/yard.encounter.ron` and `default.encounter_rules.ron`; the message `encounter-wave` in
  English and Russian; `yard_skeleton` and `yard_captain` (extend the arena's, with an `EncounterRole`). `encounter.scenario` and the stand
  scenario `stand/encounter.scenario`; the reference replay `encounter.json`. The other reference replays are unchanged except
  `region.json` and `npc_navmesh.json`, which read the tile kind whole and were recorded again for the three new tile definitions (only
  their `hash`, `content` and `check_parts` lines changed). `fate::boss_phases` runs before the encounter step.
- Hit stop (C2, port gap G3), the picture's and the real-time clock's, never the simulation's (no tick is lost or added; the `hitstop` kind is not simulation content, so content hashes and the reference replays do not change): the new module `shards_hitstop` (`shards::hitstop`, optional: `--without hitstop`). Content `*.hitstop.ron` (`HitstopDef`, one per game): an ordered list of rules over the combat events (`Damaged`, `Blocked`, `Parried`, `PerfectEvaded`, `GuardBroken`, `Staggered`; `weight_at_least`, `reaction_at_least`, `cause`, `action`, `ignored`), the first match gives a hold in seconds, with `max_seconds` (at most 0.5) and `cooldown`; the game writes its own ladder, the engine has no names from any game. Every machine that sees combat events (a client gets them from its host) triggers `HitStopped` and journals `hitstop: <rule> <s> s (<event>) on eN, eM` as `JournalKind::Local`; the machine then holds what suits it: alone with a picture (window or `--offscreen`, real time) the new `ai_core::ClockHold` keeps the virtual clock at speed 0 for the real time of the hold (not a pause: no pause menu, no muted sound, the gate does not refuse; zero ticks run inside it; `sim::speed()` reports the player's speed and `sim::set_speed` during a hold sets what the clock comes back to; `sim::step` ends a hold; new `sim::is_driven`), in co-op (host and client) no clock hold at all, only `ai_core::PresentationHolds` (by `AiId`) that the animation bridge reads (`render::bridge::drive` pauses the body's `AnimationPlayer`), and headless, `--paused`, stepped, a replay playing back, a stand's driven clocks and a loading gate hold nothing. Overlapping holds merge by the later end. Settings `accessibility.hit_stop` (0..1, 0 is off) and `accessibility.hit_stop_local_only`. Tools `hitstop` and `hitstop_play`, the `hitstop` field in `ai/status`, a developer menu item. The engine rule `hitstop.no_ticks_inside` (a hold consumes zero ticks) stops the game if a tick runs inside a clock hold; `sim::set_tick` (a snapshot, a replay start) ends a hold. Sandbox: `content/fx/sandbox.hitstop.ron` (a neutral ladder 0.06-0.10 s), a slider in its settings menu, `scenarios/hitstop.scenario`, `tests/sandbox/hitstop.rs` (a replay recorded while the clock was held verifies identical with hit stop off, headless and in a window that plays it). A co-op picture hold freezes the body's whole drawn pose (position and rotation as well as the animation) on every machine that draws it, then blends back to the live pose over as long as the hold lasted (`PresentationHolds::drawn`, asked by `shards_motion` render interpolation and by `shards_net` remote interpolation; no simulated `Transform` changes). A rule can name `involves_local: true` (only blows the local player is in), so a fight between others does not stutter the solo clock; `hitstop_play seconds=` keeps the table's ceiling.
- Rules, slice C3.1a (port gap G10, rule modifiers and difficulty): the new module `shards_rules` (`shards::rules`, a module every game runs, before `ActionPlugin`). Content `*.rule_set.ron` (`RuleSetDef`, one per mode: by class `incoming_damage`, `outgoing_damage`, `max_health`, `max_poise`, `stamina_cost` rules over action tags and ids, `perfect_bonus`, the max of named `charges`; `attack_tokens` (`shift`, `min`) and `attack_rest` for every director) and `*.rules.ron` (`RulesDef`, the one definition `default`: `modes` in order, `start`, `classes`, a `session` table by class and session size, the Fluent texts `blocked` and `host_only`), validated as a whole (factors above 0 and at most 10, bonus 0..1 s, cost rules without shared tags, charges 1..255 with names of 1..64 characters, the same charges in every rule set, token shift −8..8 and `min` 0..64, no `min` without a shift, one `rules` definition, known modes and classes with the closest name, session rows that give a column), and the prefab check `rule_class` for `RuleClass` (a value that does not read is reported when no type registry reads it). `ActiveRules { mode, generation, players }` is a snapshot resource with the prepare `rules` (after `content`: a snapshot without it removes it, a co-op host sets it neutral, a refused load puts it back) and the optional replay check `rules` (absent without rules or while neutral, so a game without rules hashes and saves as before: the fourteen reference replays of the sandbox and the template's two do not change); a new world plays `start` (`OnEnter(InGame)`, the public set `RulesSystems::Start`), the main menu sets it neutral on a host and removes it alone and on a client (a client's copy is the host's), a region trip keeps it, a content change adds to `generation` and falls back to `start` with a warning and `RulesChanged { cause: Content }` when the mode is gone. `count_session` in `RulesSystems::Apply` (in `SimSet::Input` before the engine's other sets there, `WorldSystems::Zones` and `CoreSystems::FollowOrders`, while `ActiveRules` exists; a game's own `SimSet::Input` system that reads the rules goes `.after(RulesSystems::Apply)`) counts the distinct players `SessionFilter` seats, through a query state it keeps. Also `RuleClass`, `RuleScaled`, `RulesGuards` (`add_rules_guard`, empty for now), the read API for the hook sites (`class_of`, `active_set`, `session_factor`, `incoming`, `outgoing`, `health_factor`, `poise_factor`, `RulesView`, `charge_rules`, `token_limit`, `rest_factor`; neutral without rules), the tools `rules` (with `playing`, the mode in play) and `rules_set` (`mode=` or `cycle=±1`, host only: the route refuses a client, and so does the tool where no forwarder is set; out of combat unless `force` from a dev session; a refusal of its own while a region loads; a refusal records nothing), the condition `rules_changeable`, `ai/status` → `rules`, the developer menu's "Next rule set" (it says why it does nothing in a game without rules or on a client), the event `RulesChanged` and its journal line, and the engine rule `rules.mode_known` (with a control test; 25 engine rules). No hook site reads the rules yet (C3.2-C3.5); the guard `npc_engaged`, the save section, the UI sources and the net wiring come with C3.1b. Tests: `shards_rules` `src/` (23) and `tests/rules/` (13). A test of `shards::run` (`verify_names_the_components_the_checks_leave_out`) now orders its own two `OnEnter(InGame)` systems: they were unordered, and the new exclusive `start_rules` changed the order the schedule sorted them in. The developer menu has a section `rules` after `world` (42 entries in the sandbox). Manual: "Rules (C3)".
- Rules, slice C3.1b (port gap G10): the rules module is wired to the rest of the engine. The NPC module registers the rules guard `npc_engaged` (`add_rules_guard`): the rules change only out of combat, and an active NPC with a target in a fighting state (`chase`, `attack`, `wait_token`) is combat, a partner's fight too; the reason names the first such NPC in `AiId` order (``e6 `skeleton` is fighting (`chase`, target e5) [npc_engaged]``). The test the condition `npc_engaged` makes is now one public function, `shards::npc::ai::engaged_npc`, which both use. The umbrella adds the world save section `rules` (`RulesSave`, version 1, after `region`; written in every game's world saves, `{"mode": null}` where the game has no rules; applying a null mode or a file without the section does nothing at all; another saved mode sets it, adds 1 to `generation` and triggers `RulesChanged { cause: Load }`; a mode the game's content lacks is not refused, the journal warns and the mode in play stays; a mode that is not a name is refused), the UI sources `rules.mode`, `rules.name[:mode]`, `rules.summary[:mode]` and `rules.blocked`, `net_event::<RulesChanged>()` (a guest's HUD and journal see the change) and the net `SessionFilter` (a body that waits for its player's rejoin, `NetLeft`, stops counting toward the session size at the leave), and `RuleClass` in the prelude. `shards_rules::load_mode` is what the section's apply calls. `--difficulty` stays a game's flag (a recorded `rules_set force=true` as the agent `cli`): the engine has none. Saves change shape, replays do not: every world save gains the section, `saves.rs`' registry list gains `rules`; the fifteen reference replays of the sandbox and the two of the template verify identical. Tests: `crates/shards/tests/rules_save.rs`, and `engaged_npc` in `shards_npc` `tests/npc/fsm.rs`.
- Rules, slice C3.2 (damage): `DamageCtx::rules` is the target class's `incoming_damage`, multiplied into `damage` in `modifiers` after `DamageTaken` and before `Armor` (a hit, a block's chip and a guard break; not poise, not a block's stamina cost, not a parry, an evade or god mode), and a damage hook that rebuilds from `base` folds it in. `resolve` applies the credited body's outgoing factor (`outgoing_damage` × the session row's) after the hooks to the health and poise damage of a hit, a block and a guard break, so it follows a combat object's owner and a hook's `credit`. New `deal_ruled_damage` (`deal_damage` with the target's incoming factor, for a game's burn or chain damage); `deal_damage` is documented as never scaled. A factor of 1 is never multiplied in: a game without rules, and the sixteen reference replays, are unchanged. `shards_action` depends on `shards_rules` and `ActionPlugin` adds `RulesPlugin` when it is missing. Tests: `shards_action/tests/combat/rules_damage.rs` (12). Manual: "Rules (C3)", "Damage".
- Lock-on as a simulation module (C6), opt-in with one content file: without a `lock_on` definition the camera, the `lock_on` tool and every lock behave as before (the template has none; its replays do not change). With one:
  - **Content kind `lock_on`** (`*.lock_on.ron`, id `default` (another id is a content error), hot reload, validated; `shards_motion::camera::lock`, `LockOnDef`): `range` (30 m, 3D between positions), `weights` of the scored pick (`distance` 1, `screen` 1, `view` 0.5, `danger` 0.5, `current` 0.3; lower scores win), `aim_height` 0.6 and `eye_height` 0.9 (fractions of the body's collider: one lock point per body), `obstruction_s` 2.0 (counted in ticks, 120 at 60 Hz; `None` turns the rule off), `switch_dead_band` 0.05 rad, `switch_min_distance` 0.1 m, `frustum` (pick inside the camera's frustum at the player's base field of view, never what `CameraOffsets::fov_add_deg` adds; off: the old cone), `wheel_threshold` 0 (a scroll of this much or less in a frame counts as the wheel standing still; 0 takes every turn).
  - **The rules as plain functions** shared by the camera and the action module: `lock_score`, `pick_lock`, `switch_lock` (the signed angle around up from the direction to the current target to a candidate's, measured from the one locking; a side needs more than the dead band; the nearest angle wins, then the lower `AiId`), `signed_bearing`, `angle` (through `ops::atan2`), `body_point`, `in_frustum`, `sight_clear`, `lock_candidates`, `switch_target`; `LockHooks` (who may be locked on to, who is dangerous), which the action module fills in (alive, and `LockOnTarget` or a hostile fighter; an attack whose hit zone would land on the locker).
  - **The check** (`shards_action::lock`, `LockSystems::Check`, `SimSet::Cleanup` after `CoreSystems::ClearPresses`, so after the deaths and the engine's despawns of the tick, such as the co-op body whose rejoin grace is over; in `AiId` order; every body's `Intent.target`, an agent-driven monster's too): a lock is lost when its target is gone, dead, out of range or hidden from the locker's eye behind the world for `obstruction_s` in a row; a lock on something the locker may no longer lock on to (an ally, by the pick's own rule) is let go of (`e5 lock released: e6`); the count is the component `LockSight` (target, ticks, seconds), on the locker only while its target is hidden (replay, snapshot); the journal says why: `e5 lock lost: e6 dead`, `gone`, `out of range (31.0m > 30.0m)`, `obstructed 2.0s`. The engine rule `action.lock_on_valid` (no lock on a missing or dead entity after a tick, with a control test; a game system that despawns a body that may be locked on to runs before the check, as the sandbox's corpse and layout clean-up now do; twelve engine rules with B2a's, the opt-in `voxel.body_in_solid` aside).
  - **Switching**: the press `lock_switch` (`input_names::LOCK_SWITCH`, `dir.x` the side) is used up by `LockSystems::Switch` (`SimSet::Act`, before `MotionSystems::Intent`), with or without a definition; the camera sends it for a look flick (C7's `FlickState`, only when the rule finds somebody there) and for the mouse wheel (`Binding::MouseWheel`, the standard map's `lock_switch`, inert without a definition; toward the player is right; one switch per turn of the wheel, `FlickState::wheel`). `lock_switch` is read as an axis (a button binding is refused by `rebind`, the controls settings and the content check of input maps, with an error naming the wheel, a key pair, a stick and a gamepad axis). The `lock_on` tool takes `side=left|right`, refuses a dead target, one beyond the range, an ally or anything else the pick would never name, and a body with no position, and without `target` picks by the score among what the body sees from its eye. Journal lines: `e5 locks e6` and `e5 lock released: e6` (the tool, `LockTaken`, `LockReleased`; the camera writes them as local lines), `e5 locks e7 (switched right from e6)`.
  - **The camera** with a definition picks by the score among what it sees (the frustum or the cone, a clear ray from its eye) and lets go of a lock only when the player cancels it: the simulation does the rest, with a window or without.
  - **The AI layer**: the describer `lock e6`, `lock e6 (obstructed 0.8s)` while hidden.
  - **Co-op**: `Intent.target` and the `lock_switch` press are input; the check and the switch run where the simulation runs. `InputCapture::adopt_target`: a client takes over a lock the host changed itself (lost by its check, moved by a switch or an order) once the host has every held state of the client, so the client's next state does not lock the host's body on the old target again; stand scenario `scenarios/stand/lock_on.scenario`.
  - Sandbox: `content/combat/lock.lock_on.ron` (the defaults written out), scenario `lock_on.scenario` (a lock lost to a death, to a pillar after 2 s with the count starting again after a break, to the range; switches right and left by the rule); `reactions.scenario` locks again after its pillar section (both locks are lost behind the pillar now), and the reference replays are recorded again (the content hash; `reactions.json` also by that pillar section). Tests: `camera::lock` (7), `camera::math` (1 new, the wheel), `tests/motion/camera.rs` (2 new), `camera_content.rs` (1 new), `shards_action/tests/combat/lock.rs` (9; among them a target despawned in `SimSet::Cleanup` before `ClearPresses` is let go of on that tick, and the same despawn after the check is reported by the rule), `shards_net::inputs` (1 new), the sandbox's control test of `action.lock_on_valid`. Manual: "Lock-on rules (C6)".
- Voxel regions (`shards_voxel`, G1, A2c): a region kind of its own behind the same region API as tiles. `shards_world` gains
  `Layout::Volume` (`"volume"`) and a registry of region kinds (`RegionKind`, `app.add_region_kind`, `WorldGen::kinds`,
  `kind::generation_problems`); the manifest gains `steps` (the steps with versions that made it, written only when set, so other
  manifests keep their bytes). The content kind `voxel_region` (`*.voxel_region.ron`: size, chunk, palette, version, anchor,
  attempts, steps and params, checks, required `marks`, `markers`, `spawn`, `water.surface_drop`, `sea` with an optional
  `current`, `near_edge`, `check_seed`, `fallback_seed`, `active_radius`; neutral defaults: no sea, no current, no drop),
  validated against the game's registered steps and checks (the content kinds' context, `ContentKinds::set_context`,
  `Refs::context`). `regen`, `change_region` and `region_load` build it: the terrain field, a water volume per water box, four
  sea boxes, `Current` on the water, the markers, then the points of interest from the steps' marks; `VoxelTerrain` holds the
  volume, palette, field and hash. A manifest made by another step version is refused, naming the step (saves, snapshots,
  `region_load`). The optional snapshot and replay check `voxel.volume` (absent outside a voxel region); the in-process rebuild
  cache (`SHARDS_VOXEL_CACHE=off`, `VoxelSettings`); hot reload (`ContentAppliedHooks`, `on_content_applied`: a changed
  definition makes the region again for its seed, a colour does not); `--check-content` generates each voxel region for its
  `check_seed` and `fallback_seed`; the worldgen report's `totals:` line (`Generated::counts`). `Current`, `current_at` and
  `edge_distance` in `shards_motion` (nothing moves bodies by it yet); `PhysicsWorld::wide_colliders`. Tools `sea_current_at`
  (player) and `voxel_info` (dev, and in the developer menu; its build time only where timing is reported); conditions
  `in_water`, `in_current` and `near_grid_edge`, registered when the app is built and answering in a region of any kind; the
  journal line `region loaded: <id> seed N vV (X x Y x Z, N water boxes, …)`. (Superseded by A2e below: a voxel region has a navmesh now.) Until the navigation bake, a voxel region had no
  navmesh: `npc_state`, `nav_path` (refused: `use path_to`), `nav_info` and `nav_bake` say so, and the journal line
  `navmesh: none for this region` is an event there, not a warning. A voxel region's markers carry the `TileId` of their
  chunk, as a tile's do; `RegionManifest::area`. A voxel manifest whose grid is not the one its definition makes (size, chunk
  edge, corner, a point outside, tiles or links) is refused before anything is built, as is a mark a step makes off the
  volume; a chunk must be as tall as the volume, and a sea reach at most 100 km (`MAX_SEA_REACH`). A content-applied hook
  returns the line it adds to the call's answer (`fn(&mut World) -> Option<String>`): `content_apply` says that a region was
  made again. `VoxelPlugin` added before `WorldPlugin` panics when the app is finished, naming the order. `Layout::Volume`
  needs no save `FORMAT` bump: a save of a tile region keeps its bytes, and an older build cannot read a volume's manifest (its parser says `unknown variant volume`).
  Snapshot checks may be optional (`add_optional_snapshot_check`). Manual: "Voxel regions (A2c)", with what differs when a
  region of tiles is swapped for a volume.
- Sandbox: the voxel arena (`regen region=voxel_arena seed=7`), a voxel region of 256 x 32 x 256 cells north of the arena,
  made by the sandbox's own steps (`island`, `lake`, `flat_arena`, `arena_pieces`, `cave`, `trees`, then the stock `fill_water`;
  `src/gameplay/terrain.rs`) and checks (`arena_flat`, `spawn_on_ground`, `pool_depth`, `cave_open`) from
  `content/world/arena.voxel_region.ron` and `terrain.voxel_palette.ron`. The scenario `voxel_arena.scenario` (steps, a ledge, the pool, the
  overhang, the cave, the current and the conditions; no NPC chase, no `nav_path`) is the new reference replay `voxel_arena.json`;
  `--worldgen-report voxel_arena --seeds 1..1000` runs in CI and 10000 seeds every night. `bounds` gains `past_grid` (meters past a
  region's grid that still count: the arena's sea). The other reference replays were recorded again for the new content: only their
  content hash changed.
- Voxel navigation (A2e): a voxel region's navmesh is baked in memory when the region loads, one bake per chunk and body class, from the
  voxel field and the region's water alone, sampled at the chunk's place in the world (`Sampler` takes a frame, the region's corner and the
  chunk's first column; a tile's own bake keeps its literal `(i + 0.5) * cell`, so no tile bake changes). The chunks of every body class that are not
  kept are baked at once on the compute task pool and put back in chunk order (the result does not depend on which thread finished first), kept in
  `NavBakes` under a `ChunkKey` (region, field and water hash, chunk size, chunk, body class, settings) and in a process-wide cache (so snapshot
  loads and heavy-mode twins bake nothing twice; `SHARDS_VOXEL_CACHE=off` turns it off), and stitched across every chunk edge into the same
  `NavRegion` a region of tiles has: the stitch of a volume also makes the diagonal moves across a seam (so the chunks are the grid baked in one piece), and a
  route's tile corridor is widened by the chunks around it. Only machines that simulate bake: a co-op client says `not baked: this machine does
  not simulate`. `nav_info` gains `bake` (`in memory, 16 chunks, baked in N ms`), `nav_path`, `path_to`, NPC routes and `npc_state`'s
  `nav.routes` (`navmesh, body agent`) answer on a voxel region as on tiles, `nav_bake` and `--bake-nav` say a voxel region is not made of files, and
  `--check-content` asks for no bake file of it. The journal line is `navmesh: voxel_arena seed 7 v1 (16 chunks, 250097 nodes, 16961 links across edges, 0 navlinks)`
  (no longer `navmesh: none for this region`). New in `shards_npc`: `bake_volume`, `prebake_volume`, `Cut`, `ChunkKey`, `VoxelBake`, A corner where four chunks meet is linked once (its diagonals were made by two edge rows). The tile graph holds the diagonal stitches (their ends are graph nodes, as a hop's and a navlink's are), so a route in a region too large for the full search is not refused where the components say two areas meet only across a corner (test `the_tile_graph_holds_every_diagonal_stitch_the_components_count`).
  `NavBakes::chunks` and `isolated`. Tests: the chunks stitched against the island (and a small volume with a step, a tunnel's mouth and a lake on the
  seams) baked in one piece, node for node and move for move, and 64 routes of the same length; the bake the same on every run; the cache
  keys; carving; a client that does not bake. The sandbox gains `voxel_nav.scenario` (`nav_info`, `nav_path` through the cave and up the steps, the large
  body class refused in the tunnel, a skeleton that chases the player up the steps and into the cave) and the reference replay `voxel_nav.json`
  (18 reference replays in all with B5's `downed.json`); the others are byte for byte as they were. Dev profile on a loaded machine (CPU 9-100% busy while sampling; not idle, not `--release`): the sandbox island bakes in 0.65-0.85 s for one body class (16
  chunks of 64 m on 8 workers 327-466 ms), 1.2-1.5 s for both, 2.7-3.0 s in one piece on one thread (170-190 ms a chunk; the design expected about 50 ms: not met, not re-measured idle). A route on it takes 52-55 ms corner to corner, 2-3 ms for 12 m. A world keeps only the chunk bakes of the volume it has now. Manual: "Voxel regions (A2c)", "Navigation".
- Render interpolation (C8), presentation only (no game component, content hash or replay changes): a body is drawn between its last two ticks instead of at the last tick's pose, so a 144 Hz display is not shown 60 Hz steps. `shards_motion::interp`: `Interpolated` (on the visual child of a `CharacterController` body, added by the engine; `NotInterpolated` opts out) keeps the previous and current tick pose captured in `FixedLast` (`InterpSystems::Capture`); in `PostUpdate` before transform propagation (`InterpSystems::Blend`) the child's local transform is moved by `lerp/slerp(prev, cur, overstep_fraction) - cur` (`visual_offset`), and the body's own `Transform` is never written. The follow camera follows the same blended pose (`shown_translation`). The history starts over on a jump: `CameraTuning::body_snap_distance` (content, default 4 m per tick), a `MotionState::placed` mismatch, a body moved between ticks, a gap in the tick counter. Does nothing without a picture, with the clock paused, and on a co-op client for bodies it does not predict (`shards_net` already blends those). New setting `graphics.interpolation` (default `true`); off draws exactly the old picture. Tests: pose blend at 0, 0.5 and 1, resets, the setting, pause and co-op in `shards_motion`; a replay verifies identically with it on, off and headless. Review fixes: blended children are put back at rest in `FixedFirst` (`restore_rest`) so the simulation reads the game's own child transform; parented bodies are not blended; `adopt_visuals` uses `try_insert`.
- Camera as content and effect hooks (C7), presentation only (no simulation change; content hashes and the reference replays do not change, since the sandbox has no `camera` definition): the camera's numbers are no longer constants in `systems.rs` but the resource `CameraTuning`, the content kind `camera` (`*.camera.ron`, id `default`, hot reload, ranges validated): pitch limits, follow rate, release factor, lock framing (yaw and pitch rate, fixed pitch target or the clamped line pitch, minimum horizontal distance), the arm (sphere radius, minimum distance, length-follow rate) and the flick (mouse pixel threshold, decay and cooldown, stick threshold and re-arm level), the trauma decay and amplitudes. Defaults are the old values, so a game without a definition sees no change. `CameraOffsets` (`fov_add_deg`, `focus_dip_m`, `pitch_min`/`pitch_max`) is read by `orbit_camera` after the base values and written by the game's systems (sprint field of view, landing dip, finisher punch, swim cap); the writer owns its value, the camera keeps none of it; `CameraSystems::Orbit` orders writers. After a wall lets go of the arm it grows back smoothly at `arm.follow_rate` (it still shortens at once); every smoothing is the exact exponential through `ops::exp`; new pure helpers `critically_damped_step`, `pitch_limits`, `lock_framing`, `arm_reach`, `arm_length`, `shake_offset_with` and `FlickState` in `camera::math`. `ControlState` gains `look_pointer_px()` and `look_stick()` for the flick. Tests: `camera::math` (15 new), `camera::tuning` (3), `tests/motion/camera_content.rs` (4), `camera.rs` (6 new), `input.rs` (2 new, among them: the walk is turned by the stored yaw, not by a shake). Manual: "Camera numbers and offsets (C7)".
- Stick look (C9): `Binding::Stick` gains optional `scale_x` / `scale_y` (per-axis scale, `scale` where absent) and `curve` (exponent on the magnitude after the radial dead zone, default 1.0 = linear). Unserialized while default, so old `input_map` files, saved controls strings and the reference replays are unchanged; the text form of `rebind` and `controls` carries them (`Stick(stick: Right, deadzone: 0.2, scale_x: Some(3.2), scale_y: Some(2.0), curve: 1.6)`). A bad `curve` or a non-finite scale is a content error that names the file. The same check runs on `rebind` and on a saved controls string, so a NaN never reaches `Intent`.
